> अस्मान्मादयितुं यानि संति तान्यस्मानुप यंतु । तथा सहस्रिणो वाजा गमनवंतश्चतुष्पाद्रूपाः पशवोऽपि नोऽस्मानुप यंतु । समीपं प्राप्नु वंतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, your modes of protection, are a thousand, meaning countless; "may they come to us" is to be supplied everywhere. Likewise, O Harivat: the Haris are Indra's two horses, and Indra is their owner. Your foods, most greatly desired (गूर्ततमाः (*gūrtatamāḥ*) = "most eagerly sought"; इषः (*iṣaḥ*) = what is to be sought, foods), are a thousand, that is, many. Likewise a thousand *rāyas*, that is, countless riches such as jewels and pearls, all that exist to gladden us (मादयध्यै (*mādayadhyai*) = to gladden us), may come to us. Likewise may thousands of वाजाः (*vājāḥ*), that is, moving four-footed animals, come to us, that is, reach our neighbourhood.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- ते (*te*)—your
- ऊतयः (*ūtayaḥ*)—acts of protection
- सहस्रं (*sahasraṃ*)—by the thousand
- नः (*naḥ*)—for us
- उप यंतु (*upa yaṃtu*)—may they approach
- हरिवः (*harivaḥ*)—O Indra, possessed of the horses named Hari
- गूर्ततमाः (*gūrtatamāḥ*)—(most strengthening) most excellent
- सहस्रं (*sahasraṃ*)—immeasurable
- इषः (*iṣaḥ*)—(many kinds of) foods
- (नः उप यंतु (*naḥ upa yaṃtu*)—may they approach us)

### Printed p. 74
- (likewise) सहस्रं रायः (*sahasraṃ rāyaḥ*)—countless riches, jewels, pearls and the like
- मादयध्यॆ (*mādayadhyè*)—to gladden us
- (नः उप यंतु (*naḥ upa yaṃtu*)—may they approach us)
- (likewise) सहस्रिणः (*sahasriṇaḥ*)—thousands of
- वाजाः (*vājāḥ*)—moving animals and the like
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- (उप यंतु (*upa yaṃtu*)—may they approach)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, protect us in thousands of ways. O Indra, possessed of the horses named Hari, bestow on us the most excellent and measureless foods of many kinds for our delight, countless riches such as jewels and pearls, and thousands of cattle.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
May your thousand benefits, Indra, come to us : may a thousand kinds of abundant food come to us, lord of horses : may a thousand (sorts of) wealth come to exhilarate us : may thousands of horses be ours.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

सहस्रं ऊतयः (*sahasraṃ ūtayaḥ*)—Here Indra is addressed, and the word सहस्र (*sahasra*) is explained as अपरिमिताः (*aparimitāḥ*), "too many to count". The prayer is that Indra protect us, the sacrificers, with modes of protection beyond counting.

हरिवः (*harivaḥ*)—Yāska explains हरी इंद्रस्य (*harī iṃdrasya*) (Ni. 2-28): Indra's two horses are well known as the Haris. Since Indra possesses these horses, he is addressed by the word हरिव (*hariva*).

गूर्ततमाः इषः (*gūrtatamāḥ iṣaḥ*)—अत्यंतं गूर्णानि विषणीया अन्नानि (*atyaṃtaṃ gūrṇāni viṣaṇīyā annāni*). Because they are desired by living beings, the word इषः (*iṣaḥ*) is explained as food. इषं (*iṣaṃ*) is stated among the names of food (Ni. 3-9). The prayer is that Indra bestow on us foods without measure.

सहस्रं रायः मादयध्यै उपयंतु (*sahasraṃ rāyaḥ mādayadhyai upayaṃtu*)—अपरिमितानि धनानि मणिमुक्तादीनि अस्मान् मादयितुं यानि संति तानि अस्मान् उपयंतु (*aparimitāni dhanāni maṇimuktādīni asmān mādayituṃ yāni saṃti tāni asmān upayaṃtu*). The word रायः (*rāyaḥ*) is read among the names of wealth (Ni. 3-9). The prayer is that, by Indra's favour, all the countless wealth in the form of gems, pearls and the like, which makes us joyful and glad of heart, may come to us.

### Printed p. 75
सहस्रिणः वाजाः (*sahasriṇaḥ vājāḥ*)—गमनवंतश्च तुष्पाद्रूपाः पशवः (*gamanavaṃtaśca tuṣpādrūpāḥ paśavaḥ*). The word वाज (*vāja*) is read among the names of food (Ni. 3-9). In the Ṛk पावका नः सरस्वती वाजेभिर्वाजिनीवती । यज्ञं वष्टु धियावसुः । (*pāvakā naḥ sarasvatī vājebhirvājinīvatī | yajñaṃ vaṣṭu dhiyāvasuḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-3-10), the word वाजिनीवती (*vājinīvatī*) is explained as "possessed of food in the form of oblation, or of water", so the word वाज (*vāja*) conveys several meanings. Here, however, वाजाः (*vājāḥ*) is explained as गमनवंतः (*gamanavaṃtaḥ*), moving creatures. The prayer is that, by Indra's favour, animals such as cattle, two-footed and four-footed, that go about, may come to us without number.

Yāska (Ni. 10-8) has given the etymology of the word इंद्र (*iṃdra*). इरां दृणाति (*irāṃ dṛṇāti*): he who causes sprouts and the like to arise from seeds moistened by rain-water; इरां ददाति (*irāṃ dadāti*): he who, making food arise through rain-water, gives it to the world; इरां दधाति (*irāṃ dadhāti*): he who holds food for the favour of the world; इरां दारयति (*irāṃ dārayati*): he who impels the seed to grow in the form of sprouts and the like; इंदवे द्रवतीति ना (*iṃdave dravatīti nā*): he who comes to the sacrificial hall for the drinking of soma; इंदौ रमते इति वा (*iṃdau ramate iti vā*): he who delights in the drinking of soma; इंथे भूतानीति वा (*iṃthe bhūtānīti vā*): he who favours living beings with food and the like and makes them prosper. Having so explained, Yāska says: इदं करणादित्याग्रयणः (*idaṃ karaṇādityāgrayaṇaḥ*), that is, in the view of Āgrayaṇa Indra himself is the doer of all acts in the world; इदं दर्शनादित्यापमन्यवः (*idaṃ darśanādityāpamanyavaḥ*), that is, in the view of the pupils of Upamanyu Indra sees all things of the world directly; इंदतेर्वैश्वर्यकर्मणः (*iṃdatervaiśvaryakarmaṇaḥ*), from the root इंद् (*iṃd*), of the sense of lordship, he is one who abides in surpassing lordship; इंदे शत्रूणां दारयिता वा द्रावयिता वा आदरयिता च यज्वनां (*iṃde śatrūṇāṃ dārayitā vā drāvayitā vā ādarayitā ca yajvanāṃ*), that is, one who destroys enemies, or who destroys the enemies' wealth and himself becomes wealthy, and who is attentive to those given to sacrifice. Thus he has explained it in many ways.

##### Grammar

- ऊतयः — ऊतियूतिजूति (पा. सू. ३-३-९७) इति उदात्तः किन्नंतो निपातितः । प्रथमाबहुवचनांतरूपम् ।
- हरिवः — हरी अस्य स्तः इति हरिवान् । मतुप् आगते छंदसीरः (पा. सू. ८-२-१५) इति मकारस्य नत्वम् । संबुद्धौ उगिदचां (पा. सू. ७-१-७०) इति नुम् ।

### Printed p. 76
- संयोगांतलोपे आगते मतुवसो रु संबुद्धौ (पा. सू. ८-३-१) इति रुत्वम् । आमंत्रितनिघातस्वरः आगच्छति ।
- गूर्ततमाः — अत्यंत्यं गूर्ताः गूर्ततमाः । अतिशायने तमबिष्ठन् (पा. सू. ५-३-५५) इति तमप् प्रत्ययः । स पित्त्वात् अनुदात्तो भवति ।
- मादयध्यै — मद तृप्तियोगे इति धातुः । हेतुमति च इति णिच् । णिजंतात् तुमन् विवक्षायां तुमर्थे सेसेनसे (पा. सू. ३-४-९) इति अध्यै प्रत्ययः । एजंतत्वात् कृन्मेजंतः इति अव्ययसंज्ञा भवति । प्रत्ययस्य आद्युदात्तस्वरः भवति ।
- सहस्रिणः — सहस्रम् एषाम् अस्ति इति सहस्रिणः । अत इनिठनौ (पा. सू. ५-२-११५) इति मत्वर्थे इनि प्रत्ययः । प्रत्ययस्वरेण आद्युदात्तो भवति । **NOTE**
- यंतु — इण् गतौ इति धातुः । लोट् प्रथमपुरुषबहुवचनांतरूपम् । अतिजंतस्य परत्वात् निघातस्वरः भवति । **NOTE**
- वाजाः — वृषादिषु अंतर्भूतत्वात् वृषादीनां च (पा. सू. ६-१-२०३) इति आद्युदात्तस्वरः भवति । प्रथमाबहुवचनांतरूपम् ।

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ नोऽवो॑भिर्म॒रुतो॑ यां॒त्वच्छा॒ ज्येष्ठे॑भिर्वा बृ॒हद्दि॑वैः  ॒
> सुमायाः ।  
> अध॒ यदे॑षां नि॒युतः॑ पर॒माः स॑मु॒द्रस्य॑ चिद्द॒नयं॑त पा॒रे ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । नः । अवःभिः । मरुतः । यांतु । अच्छ । ज्येष्ठेभिः । वा । बृहत्दिवैः ।  
> सुमायाः ।  
> अध । यत् । एषां । नियुतः । परमाः । समुद्रस्य । चित् । धनयंत । पारे ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 77

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ते मरुतोऽवोभी रक्षणैः सहिता नोऽस्मानच्छाभिमुख्येना यांतु ।
> आगच्छंतु । सुमायाः । मायेति प्रज्ञानाम । शोभनप्रज्ञा एव मरुतो
> ज्येष्ठेभिर्बृहद्दिवैर्वा ज्येष्ठैः प्रशस्यतमैर्मणिमुक्तादिधनैश्च सहिता आ यांतु ।
> वाशब्दः समुच्चये । आगमने कथमेषां सामर्थ्यिति अत आह । अधाथैषां
> मरुतां यद्यस्मान्नियुत एतन्नामकाः परमा उत्कृष्टा अश्वाः समुद्रस्य चित्
> चिच्छब्दोऽप्यर्थे । समुद्रस्यापि पारे परस्मिंस्तीरे धनयंत धनधारणं वहनं
> कुर्वंति । तस्माद्रक्षणैर्धनैश्चायांत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

May those Maruts, together with their protections (अवोभिः (*avobhiḥ*)), come to us, turned towards us (अच्छ (*accha*) = in front). सुमायाः (*sumāyāḥ*): माया (*māyā*) is a name for wisdom, so the Maruts are of excellent wisdom. May they come together with ज्येष्ठेभिः बृहद्दिवैः (*jyeṣṭhebhiḥ bṛhaddivaiḥ*), or with the most praiseworthy riches, gems, pearls and the like; the word वा (*vā*) is in the sense of "and". If asked how they are able to come, he answers: अध (*adha*) means "now"; since their excellent horses, named Niyuts, carry wealth even to the farther shore of the sea (चित् (*cit*) is used in the sense of "even"), therefore they come with protections and with riches.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अध (*adha*)—then
- यत् (*yat*)—for what reason
- एषां (*eṣāṃ*)—of these Marut deities
- परमाः (*paramāḥ*)—most excellent
- नियुतः (*niyutaḥ*)—the horses named Niyut
- समुद्रस्य (*samudrasya*)—of the sea
- पारे चित् (*pāre cit*)—even on the farther shore
- धनयंत (*dhanayaṃta*)—bear wealth
- (therefore)
- (ते (*te*)) मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—those Marut deities
- अवोभिः (*avobhiḥ*)—(together with) (our) acts of protection
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- अच्छ (*accha*)—facing us
- आ यांतु (*ā yāṃtu*)—may they come
- सुमायाः (*sumāyāḥ*)—Marut deities of good wisdom
- ज्येष्ठेभिः (*jyeṣṭhebhiḥ*)—most excellent
- बृहद्दिवैः वा (*bṛhaddivaiḥ vā*)—and with riches, jewels, pearls and the like, worthy of great praise, (together)
- (आ यांतु (*ā yāṃtu*)—may they come)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Maruts, possessed of excellent wisdom, your excellent horses named Niyut are able to carry and bring the wealth on the farther shore of the sea; therefore come to face us, bringing with you riches and the like that are most excellent and praiseworthy, together with acts of protection.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
May the Maruts come to our presence with benefactions: may they who are possessed of knowledge (come) with most excellent and brilliant (treasures), since their glorious horses, the Niyutas, gather wealth even on the farther shore of the sea.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

मरुतः अवोभिः नः आयांतु (*marutaḥ avobhiḥ naḥ āyāṃtu*)—रक्षणैः सहिताः आर्स्मान् आगच्छंतु (*rakṣaṇaiḥ sahitāḥ ārsmān āgacchaṃtu*). The word मरुतः (*marutaḥ*) is read among the names of priests (Ni. 3-19), and मरुत् (*marut*) among the names of gold (Ni. 2-10). The word मरुत् (*marut*), explained as मितरानिणो वा, मितरोचिनो, वा महत् द्रवंतीति ना (*mitarāniṇo vā, mitarocino, vā mahat dravaṃtīti nā*),

### Printed p. 78
is read by Yāska among the groups of gods belonging to the middle region. Having explained it as the Marut deities, and having described the meaning in many ways, as those who make sound in accordance with their strength, who shine with lustre, and who give rain, the text expresses the wish that these Marut deities come to our sacrifice with the means of protecting us.

सुमायाः (*sumāyāḥ*)—शोभनप्रज्ञाः (*śobhanaprajñāḥ*). The word माया (*māyā*) is stated among the names of wisdom (Ni. 3-13). It is described that the Maruts have extraordinary wisdom.

ज्येष्ठेभिः बृहद्दिवैः आ यांतु (*jyeṣṭhebhiḥ bṛhaddivaiḥ ā yāṃtu*). Two meanings are given: for ज्येष्ठेभिः (*jyeṣṭhebhiḥ*), "endowed with abundant lustres"; or प्रशस्यतमैः मणिमुक्तादिधनैश्च सहिताः (*praśasyatamaiḥ maṇimuktādidhanaiśca sahitāḥ*), riches such as maṇi (gems) and muktā (pearls), worthy of praise. The wish is that the Maruts come together with these.

नियुतः परमाः समुद्रस्य चित् पारे धनयंति (*niyutaḥ paramāḥ samudrasya cit pāre dhanayaṃti*)—*etannāmakāḥ utkṛṣṭāḥ aśvāḥ samudrasyāpi parasmiṃstīre dhanadhāraṇaṃ vahanaṃ kurvaṃti*. It is described that the most excellent horses called Niyuts bring wealth from the other shore of the sea; and since the Maruts have such horses in abundance, the prayer is that they come to our sacrifice with riches. The word समुद्र (*samudra*) is read among the names of the atmosphere (Ni. 2-10). On the doubt whether the word समुद्र (*samudra*) denotes the well-known earthly sea, filled with water, or the atmosphere, Yāska removes the doubt: having raised the objection तत्र समुद्र इत्येतत् पार्थिवेन समुद्रेण संदिह्यते (*tatra samudra ityetat pārthivena samudreṇa saṃdihyate*), he explains: समुद्रः कस्मात् समुद्रवंत्यस्मादापः समभिद्रवंतेनमापः संमोदंते अस्मिन् भूतानि समुदको भवति समुनत्तीति वा । (*samudraḥ kasmāt samudravaṃtyasmādāpaḥ samabhidravaṃtenamāpaḥ saṃmodaṃte asmin bhūtāni samudako bhavati samunattīti vā |*). The name "sea" has come to it because the waters move about in many ways in the form of waves and spray; because the waters run swiftly and gather in it; because, being filled with abundant waters, it gives delight to aquatic creatures; and because it wets everything with water. So Yāska explains, and he states as his conclusion that it is the atmosphere. That the Maruts possess horses is well known in the Yajurveda.

##### Grammar

- अवोभिः — अव रक्षणे इति धातुः । सर्वधातुभ्यो असुन् इति असुन् प्रत्ययः । नित्त्वात् आद्युदात्तस्वरः भवति । तृतीयैकवचनांतरूपम् ।
- यांतु — या प्रापणे इति धातुः । लोट् प्रथमपुरुषबहुवचनांतरूपम् । तिजंतनिघातस्वरः भवति ।

### Printed p. 79

##### Grammar

- अच्छ-निपातस्य च (पा. सू. ६-३-१३६) इति संहितायां दीर्घो भवति ।
- ज्येष्ठेभिः — अतिशयेन प्रशस्यः इत्यर्थविवक्षायाम् इष्टन् प्रत्ययः । ज्यच (पा. सू. ५-३-६१) इति ज्यादेशः । प्रकृत्यै, काच् (पा. सू. ६-४-१६३) इति प्रकृतिभावाद् अलोपो न भवति । गुणः । भिस् परे बहुलं छंदसि इति भिस ऐस्भावो न भवति । बहुवचने झल्येत् इति एत्वम् ।
- बृहद्दिवैः — बहुप्रीहौ प्रकृत्यापूर्वपदं इति पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरो भवति ।
- सुमायाः — शोभना मया येषां ते सुमायाः । नञ्सुभ्यां (पा. सू. ६-२-१७२) इत्युत्तरपदांतोदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- अध — अथ इत्यत्र छांदसो वर्णविकारो भवति ।
- नियुतः — यु मिश्रणामिश्रणयोः धातुः । क्विप् च इति क्विप् प्रत्ययः । प्रस्वस्यपिति कृतितुक् (पा. सू. ६-ऎ-७१) इति तुक् आगमः । कृदुत्तरपदप्रकृतिस्वरो भवति । प्रथमाबहुवचनांतरूपम् । **NOTE**
- धनयंत — धन शब्दात् तत्करोति तदाचष्टे इति वचनात् णिच् । छंदसि लुज् लज् लिटः इति वर्तमानार्थे लज् । प्रथमपुरुषबहुवचने झस्य अंतादेशः । शप् विकरणः । तं निमित्तीकृत्य णिचो गुणः । अयादेशः । णिचं निमित्तीकृत्य धन इत्यत्र टिलोपः । बहुलं छंदस्यमाज्‌योगेऽपि (पा. सू. ६-४-७५) इत्यडागमो न भवति ॥ २ ॥

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मि॒म्यक्ष॒ येषु॒ सुधि॑ता घृ॒ताची॒ हिर॑ण्यनिर्णि॒गुप॑रा॒ न ऋ॒ष्टिः ।  
> गुहा॒ चरं॑ती॒ मनु॑षो॒ न योषा॑ स॒भाव॑ती विद॒थ्ये॑व॒ सं  
> वाक् ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 80

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मिम्यक्ष । येषु । सुधिता । घृताची । हिरण्यनिर्निक् । उपरा । न । ऋष्टिः ।  
> गुहा । चरंती मनुषः । न । योषा । सभावती । विदथ्या इव । सं । वाक् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सुधिता सुष्टु निहिता सुष्टु हिता वा लोके धृताची धृतं क्षरणशीलमुदकमंचंती हिरण्यनिर्णिग्ति तरमणीयरूपा । निर्णिगिति रूपनाम । एवंरूपा ऋष्टिर्विद्युदाख्यायुधविशेषो ना उपरा न मेघमालेव येषु मरुत्तु सं मिम्यक्ष!
> म्यक्षतिर्गतिकर्मा । संगताभूत् । मेघपंक्तिविद्युताविमे अपि संगते इत्यर्थः ।
> मेघपंक्तिर्विद्युच्चोभे दृष्टांतेन विशेष्येते । गुहा निगूढा गुहायां वांतरिक्षे चरंती । तत्र दृष्टांतः । मनुषो न योषा मनुष्यस्य परिवृढादेर्महिषीवत् । सा यथा सुवेषांतःपुर एव मध्यॆ चरति तद्वत् । किं सर्वदैवमिति नेत्याह ।
> सभावती । सभा जनसंघः । तद्वती । वर्षकाल आविर्भवंतीत्यर्थः । तत्र दृष्टांतः ।
> विदथ्या वागिव । विदथो यज्ञः । तदर्हतीति विदथ्या प्रैषस्तोत्रादिरूपा वाक् ।
> सा यथा यज्ञसभां प्राप्याविर्भवति तद्वत् । यद्वा । विदथ्या वेदनार्हा
> विविदमानयोर्वाक् । सा यथा सभा भवति तद्वत् । सैवंरूपा येषु मिम्यक्ष ते
> मरुतो देवयजनमागच्छंत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

सुधिता (*sudhitā*) means well placed, or well disposed to the world; धृताची (*dhṛtācī*) is "drawing (*aṃcaṃtī*) ghee, that is, flowing water"; हिरण्यनिर्णिक् (*hiraṇyanirṇik*) is of very lovely form, निर्णिक् (*nirṇik*) being a name for form. Such is the ऋष्टिः (*ṛṣṭiḥ*), the weapon called lightning, and, like a row of clouds, it has joined with the Maruts: मिम्यक्ष (*mimyakṣa*) (the root म्यक्ष (*myakṣa*) meaning motion) = "has come together". The sense: the line of clouds and the lightning are united. Both are further qualified by similes. Hidden (गुहा (*guhā*)), or moving in the sky (गुहा (*guhā*)), like the wife of a man, such as a wandering mendicant, who moves about well attired in the very midst of the women's quarters. Is it always so? No: सभावती (*sabhāvatī*), having an assembly (सभा (*sabhā*) = a gathering of people); that is, they appear in the rainy season. Example: like speech (विदथ्या वाक् (*vidathyā vāk*)): विदथ (*vidatha*) is sacrifice, विदथ्या (*vidathyā*) is speech fit for it, in the form of the prāiṣa, stotra and the like, which appears when it reaches the sacrificial assembly. Or: विदथ्या (*vidathyā*) is the speech of two disputants, fit for knowing, which becomes an assembly. Such is it (the lightning) in whom it has joined: those Maruts come to the place of worship of the gods.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- येषु (*yeṣu*)—in which Marut deities
- सुधिता (*sudhitā*)—well placed
- घृताची (*ghṛtācī*)—raining water
- हिरण्यनिर्निक् (*hiraṇyanirnik*)—of lovely (golden-coloured) form
- ऋष्टिः (*ṛṣṭiḥ*)—lightning
- मनुषः (*manuṣaḥ*)—of a (superior) man
- योषा (*yoṣā*)—an adorned woman (गुहा चरंती (*guhā caraṃtī*): as she moves hidden in the women's quarters only)
- गुहा (*guhā*)—in the sky
- चरंती (*caraṃtī*)—moving
- विदथ्या (*vidathyā*)—fit for sacrifice
- वाक् (*vāk*)—speech in the form of hymns and the like (सभावती इव (*sabhāvatī iva*): as it appears in the sacrificial assembly)
- विदथ्या (*vidathyā*)—of those debating
- वाक् (*vāk*)—speech (सभावती इव (*sabhāvatī iva*): as it appears in an assembly)
- सभावती (*sabhāvatī*)—appearing in the rainy season
- उपरा ना (*uparā nā*)—as a chaplet of clouds (is mingled with the Marut deities)
- सं मिम्यक्ष (*saṃ mimyakṣa*)—has mingled well (may those Marut deities come to our sacrificial hall)

### Printed p. 81

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As the wife of a superior man, adorned, moves about only in the women's quarters, so the chaplet of clouds moves in the sky; and as sacrificial speech, hymns and the like, comes into the open only in assemblies, so it appears in the rainy season. As that chaplet of clouds mingles with the Marut deities, so the golden-coloured lightning, the cause of rain, is fittingly mingled with them. May such Marut deities come to our sacrificial hall.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
In whom the water-shedding, golden-coloured lightning, is fitly
deposited like a chaplet (of clouds) moving in the firmament like the
(splendidly-attired) wife of a man (of rank), and distinguished in assemblies
like a sacrificial hymn.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

सुहिता (*suhitā*)—सुष्टु निहिता सुष्टु हिता वा (*suṣṭu nihitā suṣṭu hitā vā*). Two senses are explained: that which brings good to the world, or that which is known to be lovely to everyone in the world.
धृताची हिरण्यनिर्णिक् ऋष्टिः (*dhṛtācī hiraṇyanirṇik ṛṣṭiḥ*)—धृतं क्षरणशीलं उदकं अंचति हितरमणीय रूपा विद्युदाख्यायुधविशेषो ना (*dhṛtaṃ kṣaraṇaśīlaṃ udakaṃ aṃcati hitaramaṇīya rūpā vidyudākhyāyudhaviśeṣo nā*). The word निर्णिक् (*nirṇik*) is stated among the names of form (Ni. 3-13). The word धृत (*dhṛta*) too is stated among the names of water (Ni. 2-24). The sense is that the Maruts produce the weapon in the form of lightning, joined with water of a flowing nature and of a form pleasing to those in the world; by their special motion the Maruts produce the weapon of lightning (aśani) in the waters.
उपरा न सं मिम्यक्ष (*uparā na saṃ mimyakṣa*)—मेघपंक्तिविद्युत् इमे अपि संगते इत्यर्थः (*meghapaṃktividyut ime api saṃgate ityarthaḥ*). The word उपर (*upara*) is stated among the names of cloud (Ni. 2-22). The Maruts bring about in the waters two kinds of change, fluidity and solidifying; thereby they make the line of clouds and the lightning in it be together; and, bearing in their bodies, which have their own form, the weapon known as ऋष्टि (*ṛṣṭi*) (aśani), they appear as a chaplet of clouds with lightning appears in the world. Thus the weapon-bearing form of the Maruts is compared to the form joined with cloud and lightning. Yāska—
### Printed p. 82
आ विद्युन्मद्भिमरुतः स्वर्क्कै रथेभियार्त ऋष्टिमद्धिरश्वपर्णैः । आ वर्षिष्ठया न इ षा वयो न पप्तता सुमायाः ॥ (*ā vidyunmadbhimarutaḥ svarkkai rathebhiyārta ṛṣṭimaddhiraśvaparṇaiḥ | ā varṣiṣṭhayā na i ṣā vayo na paptatā sumāyāḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-88-1)
Quoting a Ṛk that teaches this same idea, he explains the manner in which the Maruts come, bearing their weapon and mounted on chariots. In the matter of the line of clouds moving with lightning, the phrase गुहा चरंती मनुषो न योषा (*guhā caraṃtī manuṣo na yoṣā*) is given as an illustration. Lightning is hidden in the cloud and moves in the sky; so for the word गुहा (*guhā*) two meanings are explained, a secret place and the sky. The particle न (*na*) is explained by Yāska: नेति प्रतिषेधार्थियो भाषायां उभयमन्वध्यायं (*neti pratiṣedhārthiyo bhāṣāyāṃ ubhayamanvadhyāyaṃ*) (Ni. 1-4). When न (*na*) is a word of worldly usage it conveys prohibition; in the Veda it is used in both senses, prohibition and comparison. For example: नेंद्रं देवममंसत (*neṃdraṃ devamamaṃsata*)—here न (*na*) is used in the sense of prohibition.
हृत्सु पीतासो युध्यंते दुर्मदासो न सुरायां । ऊधर्न नग्ना जरंते ॥ (*hṛtsu pītāso yudhyaṃte durmadāso na surāyāṃ | ūdharna nagnā jaraṃte ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-2-10)
Here, taking it to mean that those who have drunk liquor fight like rivals, the sense of comparison is given to न (*na*) and its use in that sense shown. Likewise in ऊधर्न नग्ना (*ūdharna nagnā*) it is used in the sense of comparison. Following this, the phrase मनुषो न योषा (*manuṣo na yoṣā*) is explained as मनुष्यस्य परिवृढादेः महिषीवत् (*manuṣyasya parivṛḍhādeḥ mahiṣīvat*), as the wife of a heroic or superior man; and सा यथा सुवेषा अंतःपुर एव मध्यॆ चरति (*sā yathā suveṣā aṃtaḥpura eva madhyè carati*): the likeness of the wife of a hero, adorned with ornaments and moving hidden within the women's quarters, is applied to the movement of the line of clouds and the lightning.
सभावती विदथ्या वाक् इव (*sabhāvatī vidathyā vāk iva*)—सभा जनसंघः । तद्वती । वर्षाकाले आविर्भवंति । विदथो यज्ञः तदर्हतीति विदथ्या प्रैषस्तोत्रादिरूपा वाक् । सा यथा यज्ञसभां प्राप्य आविर्भवति तद्वत् । यद्वा वेदनार्हा विवदमानयोर्वाक् । सा यथा सभावती तद्वत् (*sabhā janasaṃghaḥ | tadvatī | varṣākāle āvirbhavaṃti | vidatho yajñaḥ tadarhatīti vidathyā praiṣastotrādirūpā vāk | sā yathā yajñasabhāṃ prāpya āvirbhavati tadvat | yadvā vedanārhā vivadamānayorvāk | sā yathā sabhāvatī tadvat*). The fact that the line of clouds with lightning is not visible at all times is described by comparing it with the speech, prāiṣa and hymn, recited at the sacrifice. In the crowd gathered in the sacrificial assembly, the prāiṣa and hymn-speech, parts of the sacrifice, are recited only at that time. In the same way lightning and the line of clouds in the sky
### Printed p. 83
are visible only in the rainy season: this is one kind of explanation. In the second meaning, for the words विदथ्या वाक् (*vidathyā vāk*), वेदनार्हा विवदमानयोः वाक् (*vedanārhā vivadamānayoḥ vāk*): the speech of those who carry on debate to settle the meaning through examination of the prior view and the established conclusion is audible in the crowd in the middle of the assembly only at that time; so the cloud-chaplet and lightning are described as visible only in the rainy season. This is explained here by the word सभावती (*sabhāvatī*). By the sentence सैवं रूपा येषु मिम्यक्ष ते मरुतो देवयजनमागच्छंतु (*saivaṃ rūpā yeṣu mimyakṣa te maruto devayajanamāgacchaṃtu*) it is explained that the Maruts move with such lines of clouds and that they, bearing their weapon, should come to the sacrificial hall for the sacrifice.
The word विदथ (*vidatha*) is stated among the names of sacrifice (Ni. 3-19). विदथानि वेदनानि (*vidathāni vedanāni*) (Ni. 6-8) also conveys the sense विज्ञानानि (*vijñānāni*).
*hotā devo amartyaḥ* … प्रचोदर्य (*pracodarya*) **NOTE**
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-27-7)
In this example the word विदथानि (*vidathāni*) is explained as knowledge.
मा नो मित्रॊ वरुणो आर्यमायुरिंद्र ऋभुक्षा मरुतः परि ख्यन् । यद्वाजिनो देवजातस्य सत्तेः प्रवक्ष्यावो विदथे वीर्याणि । (*mā no mitrò varuṇo āryamāyuriṃdra ṛbhukṣā marutaḥ pari khyan | yadvājino devajātasya satteḥ pravakṣyāvo vidathe vīryāṇi |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-162-1)
Here the word विदथ (*vidatha*) is used in the sense of sacrifice. Sāyaṇa has explained both these senses for the word विदथ (*vidatha*) (see pages 9-13).

##### Grammar

- मिम्यक्ष — मृक्ष गतौ धातुः । लिट् प्रथमपुरुषैकवचने परस्मैपदानां (पा. सू. ३-४-८२) इति इलादेशः । धातोर्द्वित्वम् । बहुलं छंदसि इत्यभ्यासस्य संप्रसारणम् । पूर्वरूपम् । पादादित्वान्निघातस्वरो न भवति । लिति (पा. सू. ६-१-१९३) इति प्रत्ययात् पूर्वस्योदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- सुधिता — डुधाण् धारणपोषणयोः धातुः । कर्मणि क्त प्रत्ययः । व्यत्ययेन धातोर्हि आदेशे धित्वम् । गतिरनंतरः (पा. सू. ६-२-४९) इति गतेः प्रकृतिस्वरो भवति । स्त्रीत्वे टाप् ।
- हिरण्यर्णिक् — हिरण्यमिव निर्णिक् यस्याः सा । बहुव्रीहौ प्रकृत्या पूर्वपदं इति पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरो भवति ।

### Printed p. 84
- गुहा — सुपां सुलुक् (पा. सू. ७-१-३९) इति सप्तम्या लुक् भवति ।
- चरंती — चर गतिभक्षणयोः धातुः । लडर्थे शतृप्रत्ययः । स्त्रीत्वे उगितश्च (पा. सू. ४-१-६) इति जीप् । शप्‌श्यनोर्नित्यं इति नुमागमः । धातुस्वरो अवशिष्यते । **NOTE**
- सभावती — सभा अस्या अस्ति इति सभावती । तदस्यास्ति इति मतुप् प्रत्ययः । मादुपधायाश्च इति मकारस्य वत्वम् । उगित्त्वात् स्त्रीत्वे जीप् । मतुप् जीप् च पित्त्वादनुदात्तौ भवतः ।
- विदथ्या इव — विद ज्ञाने धातुः । रुदिविदिभ्यां जित् (उ. सू. ३-३९५) इत्यथ प्रत्ययः । जित्त्वविधानात् लघूपधगुणो न भवति । विदथं अर्हति इति विदथ्या । अर्हार्थे यत् प्रत्ययः । यस्येति च इति पूर्वस्याकारस्य लोपः । तित् स्वरितं (पा. सू. ६-१-१८५) इति स्वरितस्वरो भवति । स्त्रीत्वे टाप् ।
- वाक् — वच परिभाषणे धातुः । क्विब्वचिप्र— (उ. सू. २-१७०) इति रक् प्रत्ययः । तत्संनियोगेन धातोरुपधाया दीर्घः । धातुस्वरेणोदात्तो भवति ॥ ३ ॥

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> परा॑ शु॒भ्रा अ॒यासो॑ य॒व्या सा॑धार॒ण्येव॑ म॒रुतो॑ मिमिक्षुः  
> न रो॑द॒सी अप॑ नुदंत घो॒रा जु॒षंत॒ वृधं॑ स॒ख्याय॑ दे॒वाः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> परा । शुभ्राः । आयासः । यव्या । साधारण्या इव मरुतः । मिमिक्षुः ।  
> न । रोदसी इति । अप । नुदंत । घोराः । जुषंत । वृधं । सख्याय । देवाः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> शुभ्राः शोभनालंकारा अयासोऽभिगंतारो मरुतो यव्या मिश्रणशीलया विद्युता परा मिमिक्षुः । प्रकर्षेण सिंचंत्युदकसंस्थायं । साधारण्येव ।
> यथा लोके साधारण्या स्त्रीया संगता युवानो रेतो मुंचंति तद्वत् । एवं

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The शुभ्राः (*śubhrāḥ*) are the Maruts, well adorned; *ayāsaḥ*, those who approach. With the mingling lightning (यव्या (*yavyā*)) they have sprinkled forth (परा मिमिक्षुः (*parā mimikṣuḥ*)): they pour out water abundantly. साधारण्येव (*sādhāraṇyeva*): as in the world young men who have united with a common woman discharge their seed, so

### Printed p. 85

> कुर्वंतो घोरा अतिवृष्टिप्रदानेन भयंकरास्ते रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्यौ नाप
> नुदंत । अपनोदनं वर्षणरूपं तिरस्कारं न कुर्वंति । द्यावापृथिवीवचनो रोदसीशब्दः सर्वत्राद्युदात्तः । अत्र तु व्यत्ययेनांतोदात्तत्वमिति द्रष्टव्यं । यद्वा।
> रोदसा शब्देन रोदनस्वभावो रुद्रः । तस्य स्त्री रोदसीति केचिदाहुः । अपरे
> तु मरुतां स्त्रियो रोदसीति नामधेयमित्याहुः । अयमेव पक्षो युक्त
> उत्तरत्रैवं व्यवहारात् । तत्पक्षॆ रोदसी रोदस्यां ॥ जीषंतात्सप्तम्या ईकारो
> लुग्वा । ईदूतौ च सप्तम्यर्थ इति प्रगृह्यसंज्ञा !! तस्यां प्रियं धनं मुंचंति ।
> किंतु. देवा मोदयितारो मादयितारो वा मरुतः सख्याय वृधं वृद्धिं
> जुषंत । सेवंते द्यावापृथिवीपक्षे सख्याय जगतां सखिभावाय तयोर्वृद्दिं
> कुर्वंतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa glosses घोराः (*ghorāḥ*): the Maruts are terrible, fearsome through giving excessive rain. They do not अपनुदंत (*apanudaṃta*) heaven and earth (रोदसी (*rodasī*)). Here अपनोदनं (*apanodanaṃ*) means the rejection that takes the form of showering rain, and they do not practise it. The word रोदसी (*rodasī*), meaning heaven and earth, is everywhere ādyudātta, but here it should be understood as antodātta by vyatyaya.

Alternatively, by the word रोदस (*rodasa*) is meant Rudra, whose nature is to weep. Some say that his wife is Rodasī. Others say that Rodasī is the name of the wives of the Maruts. This second view alone is proper, because later usage is of this kind. On that view रोदसी (*rodasī*) is रोदस्यां (*rodasyāṃ*) (locative); the ī-ending of the locative is elided or lost, and since ī and ū in the sense of the locative are pragṛhya, it receives the designation pragṛhya. In her they release their dear wealth.

देवाः (*devāḥ*) are the Maruts, the delighters or the intoxicators. They जुषंत (*juṣaṃta*) (serve) वृधं (*vṛdhaṃ*), growth, सख्याय (*sakhyāya*) (for friendship). On the heaven-and-earth view the meaning is that they bring about the growth of the two, for the friendship of the worlds.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शुभ्राः (*śubhrāḥ*)—the radiant, well adorned ones
- अयासः (*ayāsaḥ*)—the ever-moving ones
- मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—the Marut deities
- यव्या (*yavyā*)—with the mingling one (joined with the lightning)
- साधारण्या इव (*sādhāraṇyā iva*)—like youths mingled with a common woman, who discharge their seed
- परा मिमिक्षुः (*parā mimikṣuḥ*)—they shower water abundantly (while doing so)
- घोराः (*ghorāḥ*)—the formidable Maruts, given to excessive rain
- रोदसी (*rodasī*)—heaven and earth
- न अप नुदंत (*na apa nudaṃta*)—they do not reject (but)
- देवाः (*devāḥ*)—the Marut deities, causers of joy (intoxication)
- सख्याय (*sakhyāya*)—for the friendship (of the worlds)
- वृधं (*vṛdhaṃ*)—the growth (of these)
- जुषंत (*juṣaṃta*)—they serve

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The radiant, ever-moving Maruts, as in the world youths join with a common woman and discharge their seed, mingle with their companion, the lightning, and produce rain. Though fearsome, they do not trouble heaven and earth by excessive rain. Rather, desiring friendship with these worlds, they wish for their prosperity.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
The radiant, ever-moving Maruts, have mingled with (their) associate,
(lightning), like (youths) with common women ; the formidable divinities
do not overwhelm heaven and earth (with rain), but promote their prosperity
through (friendly) regard.

### Printed p. 86

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

मरुतः यव्या साधारण्येव परामिमिक्षुः (*marutaḥ yavyā sādhāraṇyeva parāmimikṣuḥ*)—The Maruts shower water abundantly with the mingling lightning, as in the world youths joined with a common woman discharge their seed. Lightning is always by nature given to joining with a cloud different from itself, and it is natural that rain arises from a cloud with lightning. Through contact with the wind the rain-waters fall to the earth. This is described here in poetic style. Since the word for lightning is feminine it is taken as a woman, and since the word मरुत् (*marut*) is masculine it is taken as a man, and the relation between the two is described. The Maruts are शुभ्राः (*śubhrāḥ*), "possessing fine, auspicious ornaments and the like", and अयासः (*ayāsaḥ*), "moving about". As in the world young men adorned with ornaments, roaming with women and given to sport, enjoy those women, so the Maruts too are described as pouring rain-waters together with the women who have the form of lightning.

घोराः रोदसी न अपनुदंत (*ghorāḥ rodasī na apanudaṃta*)—The meaning given is that the Maruts, when they pour abundant rain from the clouds, assume a form that causes fear to others. They do not reject heaven and earth, that is, they do not show the rejection that takes the form of withholding rain. Alternatively, by the word रोदस (*rodasa*) is meant Rudra, whose nature is to weep; some say his wife is Rodasī; others say Rodasī is the name of the Maruts' wives. This view alone is proper, because later usage is of this kind. On that view रोदसी (*rodasī*) is रोदस्यां (*rodasyāṃ*); in her they release their dear wealth. The word रोदसी (*rodasī*) is listed among the names of heaven and earth (Ni. 3-22). In the verse

उताभये पुरुहूत श्रवोभिरेको दृहमवदो वृत्रहा सन् । इमे चिदिंद्र रोदसी अपारे यत् संगृभ्णा मघवन् काशिरित्ते ॥ (*utābhaye puruhūta śravobhireko dṛhamavado vṛtrahā san | ime cidiṃdra rodasī apāre yat saṃgṛbhṇā maghavan kāśiritte ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-30-5)

the meaning is explained thus: heaven and earth are denoted by the word रोधसी (*rodhasī*) because they restrain and hold within themselves many kinds of creatures (Ni. 6-1). One meaning is thus stated: though the Maruts in their fierce form give excessive rain, they do not show contempt in the form of drought towards heaven and earth. In the second meaning the word रोदसी (*rodasī*) is used in the sense "wife of Rudra, power of Rudra" (Ni. 11-50), and

### Printed p. 87

उत ग्ना व्यंतु देवपत्नीरिंद्राण्य १ ग्नाय्यश्विनी राट् । आ रोदसी वरुणानी शृणोतु व्यंतु देवीर्य ऋतुर्जनीनां ॥ (*uta gnā vyaṃtu devapatnīriṃdrāṇya 1 gnāyyaśvinī rāṭ | ā rodasī varuṇānī śṛṇotu vyaṃtu devīrya ṛturjanīnāṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-46-8)

in this verse the word रोदसी (*rodasī*) is explained as "wife of Rudra" (Ni. 11-49). Sāyaṇa too, after explaining that by the word रोदस् (*rodas*) Rudra, whose nature is to weep, is meant, teaches the other view that his wife is what the word रोदसी (*rodasī*) denotes. Further, by the sentence beginning अपरे तु (*apare tu*) he explains that, since the wife of the Maruts is spoken of by the word रोदसी (*rodasī*), it is the name of the Maruts' wives, and that this way of explaining accords with the sense later on. He says that in this explanation the form रोदसी (*rodasī*) ends in the seventh case-ending, and explains तस्यां प्रियं धनं मुंचंति (*tasyāṃ priyaṃ dhanaṃ muṃcaṃti*) as: in that woman they pour their dear wealth. Yāska, citing the verse

रथं नु मारुतं वयं श्रवस्युमा हवामहे । आ यस्मिन्तस्थौ सुरणानि बिभ्रती सचा मरुत्सु रोदसी ॥ (*rathaṃ nu mārutaṃ vayaṃ śravasyumā havāmahe | ā yasmintasthau suraṇāni bibhratī sacā marutsu rodasī ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-56-8)

explains it thus: we here invoke the chariot on which the Maruts are seated together with Rodasī, who bears excellent waters (Ni. 11-50).

देवाः (*devāḥ*)—the Maruts, the delighters or the intoxicators. Yāska explains the meaning of the word deva: "a deva is so called from giving, or from illuminating, or from shining, or from dwelling in the heavenly region" (Ni. 7-15). That is, he is a deva because he bestows abundant wealth on the sacrificers, or makes the sacrificers radiant, or shines himself, or dwells in the excellent heaven. Here the Maruts are called देवाः (*devāḥ*), and the meaning explained is that these Maruts keep the sacrificers joyful or make them full of delight.

सख्याय वृधं जुषंत (*sakhyāya vṛdhaṃ juṣaṃta*)—By this sentence it is described that the Maruts attain growth so that their friendship may come to others. Heaven and earth attain growth for the good of the world; and "for the friendship of heaven and earth" means that they attain growth in order to remain inseparable from heaven and earth. It is also described that they make heaven and earth grow by their friendship.

### Printed p. 88

##### Grammar

- शुभ्राः—शुभ दीप्तौ धातुः । स्वायितंचि—(उ. सू. २-१७०) इति रक् प्रत्ययः । कित्त्वात् लघूपधगुणो न भवति । प्रत्ययस्वरेण अंतोदात्तं भवति ।
- अयासः—अय गतौ धातुः । अयंते इति अयाः । नंदिग्रहपचादिभ्यः (पा. सू. ३-१-१३४) इति अच् प्रत्ययः । चितः (पा. सू. ६-१-१६३) इति अंतोदात्तस्वरो भवति । जस् परे आज्जसेरसुक् (पा. सू. ७-१-५०) इति तस्य असुक् आगमः ।
- यव्या—यु मिश्रणामिश्रणयोः धातुः । अच इः इति इ प्रत्ययः । धातोः गुणः अवादेशः । तृतीया एकवचनांतरूपम् ।
- साधारण्या—साधारणीशब्दः जीसंतत्वात् अंतोदात्तः । तृतीयायां यणादेशे जाते उदात्तयणोहल् पूर्वात् (पा सू. ६-१-१७४) इति विभक्तेः उदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- मिमिक्षुः—मिह सेचने । अस्य धातोः समानार्थकः मिमिक्ष धातुः । लिट् प्रथमपुरुष बहुवचनांतरूपम् । तिजंतनिघातस्वरो भवति ।
- रोदसी—द्यावापृथिवीवाचकोऽयं शब्दः आद्युदात्तः अपि अत्र व्यत्ययेन अंतोदात्तो भवति । अथवा स्त्रीत्वे जीष् आगच्छति इति प्रत्ययस्वरेण अंतोदात्तो भवति । सप्तमी परे सुपां सुलुक् (पा सू. ७-१-३९) इति तस्य ईकारादेशः । अथवा लुक् । सप्तम्यर्थे ईकारत्वात् ई दूत् च सप्तम्यर्थ्ये इति प्रगृह्यसंज्ञा भवति इति अच् परे अपि संधिर्न भवति ।
- नुदंत—णुद प्रेरणे धातुः । णोनः इति आदेः नत्वम् । छांदसत्वेन वर्तमानार्थे लज् । प्रथमपुरुष बहुवचनांतरूपम् । छांदसत्वेन अडागमो न भवति । तिजंतनिघातस्वरो भवति ।
- जुषंत—जुषी प्रीतिसेवनयोः धातुः । पूर्ववत् छांदसत्वेन लज् प्रथमपुरुष बहुवचनांतरूपम् । पादादित्वात् निघातस्वरो न भवति । विकरणस्वरः अवतिष्ठते ।
- वृधं—वृधु वृद्धौ धातुः । क्विप् प्रत्ययः । द्वितीया एकवचनांतरूपम् ।
- सख्याय—सख्युर्यः इति भावार्थे य प्रत्ययः । यस्येति च इति इकारस्य लोपः । प्रत्ययस्वरेण अंतोदात्तो भवति । चतुर्थी एकवचनांतरूपम् ।

### Printed p. 89

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> जोष॒द्यदी॑मसु॒र्या॑ स॒चध्यै॒ विषि॑तस्तुका रोद॒सी नृ॒मणाः॑ ।  
> आ सू॒र्येव॑ विध॒तो रथं॑ गात्त्वे॒षप्र॑तीका॒ नभ॑सो॒ नेत्या ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> जोषत् । यत् । ईं । असुर्या । सचध्यै । विसितस्तुका । रोदसी । नृमनाः ।  
> आ । सूर्याइव । विधतः । रथं । गात् । त्वेषप्रतीका । नभसः । न इत्या ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> रोदसी मरुत्पत्नी विद्युद्वा एतन्नामिकेयमपीमेनं मरुत्संघं
> जोषत् सेवते ॥ जुषेर्लेट्यडागमः ॥ किमर्थं । सचद्यै संगमनार्थं । कीदृशी
> सा । असुर्या । असुराः क्षेप्तारो मरुतः । तेषां स्वभूता विषितस्तुका विशिष्टकेशसंघा विप्रकीर्णकेशसंघा वा संभोगवशेन तथा नृमणा नृषु नेतृषु मरुतु
> मननवती मनुष्येषु वा वृष्टिप्रदानमनोयुक्ता त्वेषप्रतीका दीपोपक्रमा
> दीप्तावयवा । ईदृशी देवी विधतः परिचरतो मरुत्संघस्य रथमा गात् । आगच्छति ।
> व्याप्नोति । यागदेशं गंतुं रथमारुह्य वा यज्ञमागच्छति । केव । विधतः
> सूर्यस्य रथं त्वेषप्रतीका सूर्येव सूर्यपत्नीव । यद्वा । सूर्यस्य दुहिताश्विनो रथमिव सा यथारोहति तद्वत् । आगमने दृष्टांतः । नभस इत्यान ।
> अंतरिक्षादित्ययोः साधारणोऽयं नभःशब्दः नभ इति षट् साधारणानि । नै. १-४ ।
> इत्युक्तत्वात् । अत्रादित्यवचनः । तस्य गतिरिव । सा यथा शीघ्रं तद्वत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Rodasī, the wife of the Maruts, or the lightning of that name, जोषत् (*joṣat*) (serves) this company of Maruts; जोषत् (*joṣat*) has the augment aṭ of the leṭ of the root *juṣ*. For what? सचद्यै (*sacadyai*), for union. What is she like? असुर्या (*asuryā*): the असुराः (*asurāḥ*) are the throwers, the Maruts; she is their own. विषितस्तुका (*viṣitastukā*): with a distinguished mass of hair, or with a scattered mass of hair, so by the force of union. नृमणा (*nṛmaṇā*): with her mind on the leaders, the Maruts, or intent on the giving of rain to men. त्वेषप्रतीका (*tveṣapratīkā*): brilliant in beginning, with shining limbs. Such a goddess comes (गात् (*gāt*), आगच्छति (*āgacchati*), reaches) to the chariot of the serving band of Maruts; either mounting the chariot to go to the place of sacrifice, or coming to the sacrifice. Like whom? सूर्येव (*sūryeva*), like the wife of Sūrya going to Sūrya's chariot with brilliant form; or, as the daughter of Sūrya mounts the chariot of the Aśvins, so she. An illustration of her coming: नभस इत्या न (*nabhasa ityā na*). The word नभस (*nabhasa*) is common to the atmosphere and the sun, since it is said "नभ (*nabha*) is one of six common words" (Nai. 1-4) **NOTE**. Here it denotes the sun. She comes as the course of the sun is, swift.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- असुर्या (*asuryā*)—belonging to the Maruts, the scatterers (of the clouds)
- विषितस्तुका (*viṣitastukā*)—with a scattering mass of hair (and)
- नृमणाः (*nṛmaṇāḥ*)—with her mind fixed on the Marut deities, the leaders
- रोदसी (*rodasī*)—Rodasī, the wife of the Maruts (lightning)
- सचध्यॆ (*sacadhyè*)—for union
- ईं (*īṃ*)—this company of Maruts
- यत् (*yat*)—because
- जोषत् (*joṣat*)—she serves (desires)
- तत् (*tat*)—therefore
- त्वेषप्रतीका (*tveṣapratīkā*)—Rodasī, brilliant in beginning (with shining limbs)
- सूर्या इव (*sūryā iva*)—like the wife of Sūrya (mounting the chariot of Sūrya) or like the daughter of Sūrya

### Printed p. 90

- (mounting) the chariot of the Aśvin deities
- नभसः (*nabhasaḥ*)—of the sun
- इत्या न (*ityā na*)—(as his course is swift) with swift motion
- विधतः (*vidhataḥ*)—of the Maruts, restless by nature
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—the chariot
- आ गात् (*ā gāt*)—she reaches

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Rodasī (the lightning), the companion of the Marut deities who scatter the clouds, with dishevelled mass of hair and with her mind fixed on the Maruts themselves, the wife of the Maruts, desires the company of the Maruts for union. Therefore, with shining limbs, as the wife of Sūrya mounts Sūrya's chariot, or as Sūryā, the daughter of Sūrya, mounts the chariot of the Aśvins, she mounts the chariot of the Marut deities, with a speed as swift as the sun's.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Rodasi, the associate of the scatterers(of the clouds), with dishevelled
tresses, and mind devoted to her lords, wooes thcm to associate (with her):
Radiant in form, she has ascended the car of the restless (Maruts) as Surya
(ascended the car of the Ashwins), and comes hither (with the celerity) of
the sun.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

रोदसी ईं जोषत् (*rodasī īṃ joṣat*)—मरुत्पत्नी विद्युत् (*marutpatnī vidyut*) and so on: the lightning, who is the wife of the Maruts, serves the company of the Maruts. In poetic style a conjugal relation is described between the Maruts and the lightning. It is well known in the Purāṇas that there are seven kinds of Maruts (the names Āvaha, Udvaha, Saṃvaha, Pravaha, Parivaha and so on are well known for the seven Maruts). Following this, "company of Maruts" is said here. This is one kind of meaning. Another meaning is also understood here from the sentence एतन्नामिका मरुत्संघं एनं सेवतॆ (*etannāmikā marutsaṃghaṃ enaṃ sevatè*): a woman, seeing men roaming here and there with desire for pleasure, becomes attached also to their companies.

The particle ईं (*īṃ*) is explained by Yāska: अथ ये प्रवृत्तेर्थमिताक्षरेषु ग्रंथेषु वाक्य पूरणा आगच्छंति पदपूरणास्ते मिताक्षरेष्वनर्थकाः कमीमिद्विति (*atha ye pravṛtterthamitākṣareṣu graṃtheṣu vākya pūraṇā āgacchaṃti padapūraṇāste mitākṣareṣvanarthakāḥ kamīmidviti*). That is: in works in prose, particles (indeclinables) are used to complete the sentence; in works bound by metre they are used in the sense of completing the word (the foot). These are the particles कं (*kaṃ*), ईं (*īṃ*), इत् (*it*), उ (*u*). Of these, the particle ईं (*īṃ*) is used here. For it Sāyaṇa explains the meaning एनं मरुत्संघं (*enaṃ marutsaṃghaṃ*).

### Printed p. 91

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

असुर्या (*asuryā*)—असुराः क्षेप्तारो मरुतः तेषां स्वभूता (*asurāḥ kṣeptāro marutaḥ teṣāṃ svabhūtā*). The Marut deities move in keeping with their own speed. The meaning is: she who, as the property of these deities, is under their authority.

विषितस्तुका (*viṣitastukā*)—विशिष्टकेशसंघा विप्रकीर्णकेशसंघा (*viśiṣṭakeśasaṃghā viprakīrṇakeśasaṃghā*) ना संभोगेन तथा (*nā saṃbhogena tathā*). The wife of the Marut deities is one who has gathered her hair together and adorned herself, or one whose hair is dishevelled from enjoying union with her husband.

नृमणाः (*nṛmaṇāḥ*)—नेतृषु मरुत्सु मननवतीः वृष्टिप्रदानमनोयुक्ता वा मनुष्येषु (*netṛṣu marutsu mananavatīḥ vṛṣṭipradānamanoyuktā vā manuṣyeṣu*). The wife of the Marut deities is one who always meditates on the welfare of her husband; or she has a mind inclined to grant the waters of rain on behalf of men. Thus the meaning is explained.

तेषप्रतीका (*teṣapratīkā*)—दीपोपक्रमा, दीप्तावयवा (*dīpopakramā, dīptāvayavā*). The wife of the Marut deities, who begins her undertakings with radiance and whose limbs shine with lustre, climbs the chariot of the Maruts, which moves around, enveloping her on all sides, and is eager to come to the place where the sacrifice is being performed. In this matter the simile is given here in the phrase विधतः रथं सूर्येव (*vidhataḥ rathaṃ sūryeva*). Sāyaṇa explains this phrase in two ways. In the first meaning the comparison is set out thus: as the wife of Sūrya mounts Sūrya's chariot.

In the second meaning: यद्वा सूर्यस्य महिता अश्विनोः रथमिव सा यथा आरोहति तद्वत् (*yadvā sūryasya mahitā aśvinoḥ rathamiva sā yathā ārohati tadvat*). It is said by way of illustration, as the daughter of Sūrya mounts the chariot of the Aśvin deities at her svayaṃvara. This matter is set out in the following, in this book:

आ नां रथं दुहिता सूर्यस्य कार्ष्मेवातिष्ठदर्वता जयंती । विश्वे देवा अन्वमन्यंत हृद्धिः समु श्रिया नासत्या सचेथॆ ॥ (*ā nāṃ rathaṃ duhitā sūryasya kārṣmevātiṣṭhadarvatā jayaṃtī | viśve devā anvamanyaṃta hṛddhiḥ samu śriyā nāsatyā sacethè ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-116-17)

And:

आ वां रथं युवतिस्तिष्ठदत्र जुष्ट्वी नरा दुहिता सूर्यस्य ॥ (*ā vāṃ rathaṃ yuvatistiṣṭhadatra juṣṭvī narā duhitā sūryasya ‖*)

This too is said in this Ṛk. (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-118-5)

नभसः इत्या न (*nabhasaḥ ityā na*)—अंतरिक्षादित्ययोः साधारणः नभश्यब्दः अत्र आदित्यवचनः (*aṃtarikṣādityayoḥ sādhāraṇaḥ nabhaśyabdaḥ atra ādityavacanaḥ*). तस्य गतिरिव (*tasya gatiriva*). सा यथा शीघ्रा तद्वत् (*sā yathā śīghrā tadvat*). The word नभः (*nabhaḥ*) is stated (Ni. 2-13) in the middle of the six common names beginning with स्वः (*svaḥ*) and पृश्निः (*pṛśniḥ*). साधारणान्युत्तराणि षट् दिवश्चादित्यस्य च (*sādhāraṇānyuttarāṇi ṣaṭ divaścādityasya ca*)

### Printed p. 92
These six common names are explained by Yāska as synonymous words denoting the atmosphere and the Āditya (the sun). नभ आदित्यो भवति नेता भासां ज्योतिषां प्रणयोऽपि ना भन एव स्याद्विपरीतो न न भातीति । (*nabha ādityo bhavati netā bhāsāṃ jyotiṣāṃ praṇayo 'pi nā bhana eva syādviparīto na na bhātīti |*) (Ni. 2-15). सर्वासु दिक्षु भासांनेता (*sarvāsu dikṣu bhāsāṃnetā*)—he who spreads light in all directions; from this the Āditya got the name नभ (*nabha*). Or else the word भन (*bhana*) becomes नभ (*nabha*) by a transposition of letters; the word भनः (*bhanaḥ*) also becomes नभः (*nabhaḥ*). Or else न न भाति (*na na bhāti*), that is, भात्येव (*bhātyeva*): he is always luminous. By this derivation the word नभ (*nabha*) is here described as meaning the Āditya.

As the course of this Āditya is swift, so the wife of the Maruts mounts the swift-moving chariot of the Maruts and comes with the Maruts to the sacrificial hall; thus the swift movement of the Āditya is compared to her coming and so explained.

##### Grammar

- जोषत्—जुषी प्रीतिसेवनयोः धातुः । तुदादिः । लेट् प्रथमपुरुषैकवचने इतश्च परस्मैपदेषु (पा. सू. ३-४-९७) इति व्यत्ययेन तिपि आगते तस्य इकारस्य लोपः । लेटोडाट् (पा. सू ३-४-९४) इति तस्य अडागमः । छांदसत्वेन विकरणस्य लोपः । पुगंतलघूपधस्य च (पा. सू. ७-३-८६) इति धातोः लघूपधस्य गुणः । पादादित्वात् निघातस्वरो न भवति । धातुस्वरो अवतिष्ठते ।
- सचध्यै—षच समवाये । धातुः । तुमर्थे अर्थे तुमर्थे सेसेनसे—(पा. सू. ३-४-९) इति अध्यै प्रत्ययः । कृन्मेजंतः इति अव्ययसंज्ञा भवति । प्रत्ययस्य आद्युदात्तस्वरेण मद्योदात्तो भवति ।
- असुर्या—असुराणां संबंधिनी । संबंधार्थे यत् प्रत्ययः । तित्‌स्वरितं (पा. सू. ६-१-१८५) इति स्वरितस्वरो भवति । स्त्रीत्वे टाप् ।
- विषितस्तुका—विषिताः विप्रकीर्णाः स्तुकाः केशसंघाः यस्याः सा । बहुव्रीहौ पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरो भवति ।
- नृमनाः—नृषु मनः यस्या साः । परादिष्छंदसि बहुलं (पा. सू. ६-२-१९९) इति उत्तरपदाद्युदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- सूर्या—सूर्यस्य स्त्री देवता । सूर्याद्देवतायां चाब्बाच्यः (पा. सू. ४-१-४८) इति चाप् प्रत्ययः ।

### Printed p. 93
- विधतः—विध विधाने । धातुः । शतृप्रत्ययः । षष्ठी एकवचनांतरूपं । नुम् रहितस्य शतृः परे विभक्तेः आगतत्वात् शतुरनुमोनद्यजादी (पा. सू. ६-१-१७३) इति तस्य उदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- गात्—इण् गतौ । धातुः । छांदसत्वेन वर्तमानार्थे लुज् इणो गा लुजि (पा सू. २-४-४५) इति धातोः गादेशः । च्लेः सिचादेशे आगते गातिस्थाघु—(पा. सू. २-४-७७) इति तस्य लुक् । छांदसत्वेन अडागमो न भवति । तिजंतनिघातस्वरो भवति ।
- इत्या—इण् गतौ । धातुः । एति स्तुशा--(पा. सू. ३-१-१०९) इति क्यप् प्रत्ययः । ह्रस्वस्य पितिकृति—(पा. सू. ६-१-७१) इति धातोः तुकागमः । स्त्रीत्वे टाप् ।

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आस्था॑पयंत युव॒तिं युवा॑नः शु॒भे निमि॑श्लां वि॒दथे॑षु प॒ज्रां ।  
> अ॒र्को यद्वो॑ मरुतो ह॒विष्मा॒न्गाय॑द्दा॒थं सु॒तसो॑मो  ॒
> दुवस्यन् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । अस्थापयंत । युवतिं । युवानः । शुभे । निमिश्लां । विदथेषु । पज्रां ।  
> अर्कः । यत् । वः । मरुतः । हविष्मान् । गायत् । गाथं । सुतसोमः ।  
> दुवस्यन् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> युवानो मिश्रयंतो नित्यतरुणा ना मरुतो युवतिं मिश्रयंतीं
> नित्यतरुणीं वा विद्युद्रूपां रोदस्यां स्त्रीयमास्थापयंत । आस्थापयंति । देवता-

### Printed p. 94

> तेन रथे धारयंति । कीदृशीं तां । निमिश्लां । नियमेन मिश्रयंतीं पज्रां ।
> पज्रो बलं । तद्वतीं । शुभे । शोभते दीप्यत इति शुबुदकं । वृष्ट्वुदकार्थं ।
> शुभे रथ इति वा योज्यं । किं निमित्तं । विदथेषु यागेषु निमित्तभूतेषु । कदा ।
> यद्यदा हे मरुतः वो युष्मानर्कः ॥ मत्त्वर्थो लुप्यते । अर्चनसाधनमंत्रोपेतः स्तोता ना हविष्मान् प्रदेयाज्यादिहविर्युक्तः सुतसोमो
> होमायाभिषुतसोमोऽयं यजमानो दुवस्यन् । परिचरन् गाथं गातव्यं
> स्तोत्रं गायत् गायति । तदेत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The youthful Maruts, either "mingling" or "ever young", set up (आस्थापयंत (*āsthāpayaṃta*), i.e. आस्थापयंति (*āsthāpayaṃti*)) the young woman, who mingles or is ever young, the lightning-formed Rodasī, in their chariot; they carry her there as a deity. What is she like? निमिश्लां (*nimiślāṃ*): one who mingles constantly. पज्रां (*pajrāṃ*): पज्रो बलं (*pajro balaṃ*), one who possesses strength. शुभे (*śubhe*): शोभते दीप्यत इति शुबुदकं (*śobhate dīpyata iti śubudakaṃ*), shub is water, so "for the water of rain"; or शुभे रथ इति वा योज्यं (*śubhe ratha iti vā yojyaṃ*), it may be construed with "chariot", as "in the splendid chariot". For what reason? विदथेषु (*vidatheṣu*), on account of sacrifices, which are the occasion. When? यद्यदा (*yadyadā*), when, O Maruts, your अर्कः (*arkaḥ*) (the suffix denoting possession is dropped): the worshipper furnished with the mantra that is the means of worship; हविष्मान् (*haviṣmān*), provided with oblations such as those to be given and offered; सुतसोमः (*sutasomaḥ*), this sacrificer who has pressed Soma for the offering; दुवस्यन् (*duvasyan*), serving you; chants the गाथं (*gāthaṃ*), the hymn that is to be sung. गायत् (*gāyat*) stands for गायति (*gāyati*). The sense is: at that time.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (हे) मरुतः (*(he) marutaḥ*)—O Marut deities
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- हविष्मान् (*haviṣmān*)—he who has oblations such as ghee, to be offered
- सुतसोमः (*sutasomaḥ*)—he who has Soma pressed for the offering
- अर्कः (*arkaḥ*)—the sacrificer, distinguished by the mantra that is the means of worship
- वः (*vaḥ*)—you
- मवस्यन् (*mavasyan*)—serving
- गाथं (*gāthaṃ*)—the hymn that is to be sung
- गायत् (*gāyat*)—sings
- युवानः (*yuvānaḥ*)—you who are ever young
- निमिश्लां (*nimiślāṃ*)—one who mingles well (with you)
- पज्रां (*pajrāṃ*)—strong one
- युवतिं (*yuvatiṃ*)—the ever-young woman (of a mingling nature), of lightning form
- शुभे (*śubhe*)—for the sake of the water of rain (in your auspicious chariot)
- विदथेषु (*vidatheṣu*)—the sacrifices being the occasion (when you set out with the sacrifices in view)
- आ अस्थापयंत (*ā asthāpayaṃta*)—you take (her) along, seating her together with you

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Marut deities, when your worshipper (the sacrificer), having made ready ghee and other oblations and the Soma juice, recites your praises, you, ever young, set out for the sacrifices in your chariot, with the lightning-formed woman who mingles well with you, who is full of great impetus and is ever young, bringing rain as you go.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
The youthful (Maruts) have placed the young (lightning) in (their) brilliant (car), commingling (with them), and vigorous (in yielding rain) on sacrifices (being solemnized); when, Maruts, your worshipper, presenting oblations, pouring out the Soma juice, and offering adoration, chants the sacred song.

### Printed p. 95

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

युवानः (*yuvānaḥ*)—मिश्रयंतो नित्यतरुणा वा (*miśrayaṃto nityataruṇā vā*). Yāska:

युवं च्यवानं सनयं यथा रथं पुनर्युवानं चरथाय तक्षथुः । निष्ठौग्रमूहथुरद्भ्यस्परि विश्वेत्ता नां सवनेषु प्रवाच्या ॥ (*yuvaṃ cyavānaṃ sanayaṃ yathā rathaṃ punaryuvānaṃ carathāya takṣathuḥ | niṣṭhaugramūhathuradbhyaspari viśvettā nāṃ savaneṣu pravācyā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-39-4)

Here, for the word युवानं (*yuvānaṃ*), he explains the meaning: युवा प्रयाति कर्माणि मिश्रयति (*yuvā prayāti karmāṇi miśrayati*), he brings rites together. Sāyaṇa explains the meaning as: the Maruts are always in youth, and are joined with one another (those who come together).

युवतीं आस्थापयंत (*yuvatīṃ āsthāpayaṃta*)—मिश्रयंतीं नित्यतरुणीं ना विद्युद्रूपां रोदस्यां स्त्रियं आस्थापयंति देवतात्वेन रथे धारयंति (*miśrayaṃtīṃ nityataruṇīṃ nā vidyudrūpāṃ rodasyāṃ striyaṃ āsthāpayaṃti devatātvena rathe dhārayaṃti*). They have seated in their chariot the woman called Rodasī, who is always in youth, of a nature (desire) to unite with men, shining like lightning or of the form of lightning. The meaning is that they carry her so that those who worship their chariot and themselves may come to know that this woman Rodasī is of the nature of a deity.

शुभे (*śubhe*)—शोभते दीप्यते इति शुबुदुकं वृष्टुदकार्थं शुभे रथ इति वा योज्यं (*śobhate dīpyate iti śubudukaṃ vṛṣṭudakārthaṃ śubhe ratha iti vā yojyaṃ*). By the derivation "that which shines, is lustrous", the water is called shub; and the meaning is stated as "for the sake of giving water in the form of rain". Or else another meaning is described: "in their auspicious chariot".

अर्कः (*arkaḥ*)—अर्चनसाधनमंत्रोपेतः स्तोता वा (*arcanasādhanamaṃtropetaḥ stotā vā*). The word अर्क (*arka*) is stated among the names of food (Ni. 3-9) and among the names of the thunderbolt (Ni. 3-11): अर्को देवो भवति यदेनमर्चति । अर्को मंत्रो भवति यदनेनार्चंति । अर्कमन्नं भवति अर्चति भूतानि । अर्कॊ वृक्षो भवति संवृत्तः कटुकिम्ना । (*arko devo bhavati yadenamarcati | arko maṃtro bhavati yadanenārcaṃti | arkamannaṃ bhavati arcati bhūtāni | arkò vṛkṣo bhavati saṃvṛttaḥ kaṭukimnā |*). The word अर्क (*arka*) is formed from the root अर्च (*arca*), meaning to worship, to honour. As the word is formed by the derivation अर्चतीत्यर्कः (*arcatītyarkaḥ*), so it is also used when it conveys the meanings "means of worship" and "worthy of worship". Thus one kind of meaning is explained: since the praisers worship him as worthy, the deity is the meaning of the word अर्क (*arka*). It is also explained that the mantra is called अर्क (*arka*) because it is a means of worship; and that food too is a meaning of the word अर्क (*arka*), being worthy of worship, since beings prepare food while performing worship. It is explained that the word denotes a tree because of its bitter nature. Since the suffix matup that follows the word अर्क (*arka*) is dropped here, the word अर्क (*arka*) is used also for one who possesses a mantra that is a means of worship; this is the meaning stated.

### Printed p. 96
From this it is explained that the sacrificers, knowing the mantras of worship, or reciting praise, furnished with the oblations offered to Agni, having made ready Soma juice for offering in Agni, recite hymns while serving Agni.

दुवस्यन् (*duvasyan*)—परिचरन् (*paricaran*). दुवस्यति (*duvasyati*) is stated among the verbs of service (Ni. 3-13). And:

परेयिवांसं प्रवतो महीरनु बहुभ्यः पंथा मनुपस्पशानं । वैवस्वतं संगमनं जनानां यमं राजानां हविषा दुवस्य । (*pareyivāṃsaṃ pravato mahīranu bahubhyaḥ paṃthā manupaspaśānaṃ | vaivasvataṃ saṃgamanaṃ janānāṃ yamaṃ rājānāṃ haviṣā duvasya |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-14-1)

Here the word दुवस्य (*duvasya*) is explained as राध्नु हि (*rādhnu hi*). Durgācārya explains the word राध्नु हि (*rādhnu hi*) as "serve".

गाथं (*gāthaṃ*)—गातव्यं स्तोत्रं (*gātavyaṃ stotraṃ*). The word गाथा (*gāthā*) is read among the names of speech (Ni. 2-23). Here the word गाथं (*gāthaṃ*) is used; the meaning "hymn" is given for it.

##### Grammar

- अस्थापयंत—ष्ठा गतिनिवृत्तौ । धातुः । हेतुमति च इति णिच् । अर्तिह्री--(पा. सू. ७-३-३६) इति धातोः आकारांतत्वात् पुकागमः । छांदसत्वेन वर्तमानार्थे णिजंतात् लज् प्रथमपुरुषबहुवचनांतरूपं । तिजंतनिघातस्वरो भवति ।
- युवतिं—युवन् शब्दः । स्त्रीत्वे यूनस्तिः (पा. सू. ४-१-७७) इति शि प्रत्ययः । नलोपःप्रातिपदिकांतस्य इति नकारस्य लोषः । प्रत्ययस्वरेण अंतोदात्तो भवति ।
- शुभे—शोभत इति शुप् । शुभ दीप्तौ । धातुः । क्विप् प्रत्ययः । चतुर्थी एकवचनांतरूपं । सावेकाचः— (पा. सू. ६-१-१६८) इति विभक्तेः उदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- विदथेषु—विद ज्ञाने । धातुः । रुदिविदिभ्यां जित् (उ. सू. ३-३९५) इति अथ प्रत्ययः । जित्त्वातिदेशकरणात् लघूपधगुणो न भवति । प्रत्ययस्य आद्युदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- अर्कः—अर्च पूजायां धातुः । कृदाधारार्चि—(उ. सू. ३-३१०) इति क प्रत्ययः । अर्क इति मंत्रः । अत्र तद्वान् इत्यर्थत्वात् छांदसत्वेन मत्त्वर्थप्रत्ययस्य लोपः । अथवा अर्शआदिषु अंतर्भूतत्वात् मत्त्वर्थे अच् प्रत्ययः ।

### Printed p. 97

##### Grammar

- गायत्—गै शब्दे धातुः । छांदसत्वात् वर्तमानार्थे लज् प्रथमपुरुषैकवचनांतरूपम् । बहुलं छंदस्यमाज् योगेऽपि (पा. सू ६-४-७५) इति अडागमः न भवति । यद्योगत्वात् निघातस्वरः न भवति । शप् अनुदात्तः इति धातुस्वरः अवतिष्ठते ।
- सुतसोमः—सुतः सोमः येन सः । बहुव्रीहौ प्रकृत्यापूर्वपदं (पा. सू. ६-२-१) इति पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरः भवति ।
- मवस्यन्—दुवस् इति परिचरणार्थे अस्ति । कंड्वादिभ्यो यक् (पा. सू. ३-१-२७) इति स्वार्थे यक् । यगंतात् लटि आगते तस्य शत्रादेशः । यक् स्वरः अवतिष्ठते । ॥ ६ ॥

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र तं वि॑वक्मि॒ वक्म्यो॒ य ए॑षां म॒रुतां॑ महि॒मा स॒त्यो अस्ति॑ ।  
> सचा॒ यदीं॒ वृष॑मणा अहं॒युः स्थि॒रा चि॒ज्जनि॒र्वह॑ते सुभा॒गाः ॥७॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । तं । विवक्मि । वक्म्यः । यः । एषां । मरुतां । महिमा । सत्यः । अस्ति ।  
> सचा । यत् । ईं । वृषमनाः । अहंयुः । स्थिरा । चित् । जनीः । वहते । सुभागाः॥७॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> एषां मरुतां यो महिमा महत्वातिशयो वक्स्यः सर्वैः स्तुत्यः
> सत्योऽबाध्योऽमोघोऽस्ति तं प्रविवक्कि । प्रवच्चि । वर्णयामि । कथमेषां
> महिमा प्राप्त इति उच्यते । यद्यस्मादीमेतेषां संबंधिनी रोदसी वृषामणा
> वृष्ट्वादिवर्षणमनस्काहंयुरहंकारवती । जगदुपकारकर्त्रि न कदाचिदस्तीत्य
> हंकारः । स्थिरा । चित् । चिदिति पूजायां । अत्यंतमविनश्वरा ईदृशी देवी सुभागाः
> XIII 7

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: the greatness of these Maruts, their surpassing majesty, which is to be praised by all and is true, that is, unobstructable and unfailing, I proclaim, I describe. How has this greatness of theirs come about? He explains: because their Rodasī (the consort), with a mind bent on raining and so on, and haughty (the haughtiness being that she is never other than a benefactor of the world), is ever-enduring; चित् (*cit*) is in the sense of honour; she is utterly imperishable, a goddess of this kind. The people endowed with good fortune, productive by nature (or the productions of rain) …

### Printed p. 98

> शोभनभाग्योपेताः जनीर्जननशिलाः प्रजा वृष्टुत्पत्तीर्वा वहते धारयति । वृष्टा
> सत्यां सर्वे प्राणिन उत्पद्यंते । यस्मादेवं तस्मान्महिमास्ति । तं महिमानं
> सौमीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

… she upholds: when rain exists all beings are born. Since it is so, the greatness exists; that greatness I praise, is the meaning.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—by the greatness of which Maruts
- वृषमणाः (*vṛṣamaṇāḥ*)—she who has a rain-shedding mind
- अहंयुः (*ahaṃyuḥ*)—the haughty one
- स्थिरा चित् (*sthirā cit*)—the eternal one
- ईं (*īṃ*)—the lightning goddess named Rodasī, belonging to these Maruts
- सुभागाः (*subhāgāḥ*)—those endowed with auspicious fortune
- जनीः (*janīḥ*)—the progeny, productive by nature; [or सुभागाः (*subhāgāḥ*)—the cause of auspicious fortune; जनीः (*janīḥ*)—the rain, productive by nature]
- सचा वहते (*sacā vahate*)—she fully upholds
- एषां (*eṣāṃ*)—of such
- मरुतां (*marutāṃ*)—Maruts
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which
- महिमा (*mahimā*)—(greatness) majesty
- वक्म्यः (*vakmyaḥ*)—to be praised by all
- सत्यः (*satyaḥ*)—unobstructable (unfailing)
- अस्ति (*asti*)—is
- तं (*taṃ*)—that majesty
- प्र निवक्कि (*pra nivakki*)—I describe well

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

I describe well the great majesty of the Maruts, which is true and praiseworthy. Rodasī, the consort of the Maruts (the lightning goddess), whose mind is bent on producing rain, who is haughty (as being a benefactor of the world) and eternal, by this very great majesty of theirs sustains, by producing rain, the fortunate and productive people.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
I celebrate that greatness of the Maruts, which is true, and worthy of celebration, inasmuch as their highminded, haughty, and ever-enduring (bride), supports a flourishing progeny.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

महिमा वक्त्यः-महत्वातिशयः सर्वैः स्तुत्यः (*mahimā vaktyaḥ-mahatvātiśayaḥ sarvaiḥ stutyaḥ*): the abundant greatness of these Maruts is to be praised by all. And since it is सत्यः-अबाध्यः अमोघः (*satyaḥ-abādhyaḥ amoghaḥ*), that is, incapable of being obstructed by anyone and not in vain, the seer of the mantra has described it, saying "I describe this greatness."

ईं वृषमणाः अहंयुः जनीः वहते (*īṃ vṛṣamaṇāḥ ahaṃyuḥ janīḥ vahate*)—एतेषां संबंधिनी रोदसी वृष्ट्यादि वर्षणमनस्का अहंकारवती जगदुपकारकर्त्रि जननशीलाः प्रजाः वृष्टुत्पत्तीर्वा धारयति (*eteṣāṃ saṃbaṃdhinī rodasī vṛṣṭyādi varṣaṇamanaskā ahaṃkāravatī jagadupakārakartri jananaśīlāḥ prajāḥ vṛṣṭutpattīrvā dhārayati*). The goddess belonging to these Maruts (the goddess famed by the name Rodasī)

### Printed p. 99
has a mind attached to pouring rain; she has a sense of self-importance in doing good to the world; ever worthy of worship, she upholds the people, who are of a nature to be born. Or, since when rain occurs all beings are born, she causes rain to be produced and upholds all. Therefore, since the greatness of the Maruts is manifested through this goddess, it is said here that the greatness of these Maruts is described.

स्थिराचित्—चिदिति पूजायां । अत्यंतमविनश्वरा (*sthirācit—ciditi pūjāyāṃ | atyaṃtamavinaśvarā*). Yāska, saying that the particle चित् (*cit*) is used in many kinds of meaning (चिदित्येषोऽनेककर्मा (*cidityeṣo 'nekakarmā*)), gives examples: in आचार्यश्चिदिदं ब्रूयादिति पूजायां, दधिचिदित्युपमार्थे, कुल्माषांश्चिदाहरेत्यवकुत्सिते (*ācāryaścididaṃ brūyāditi pūjāyāṃ, dadhicidityupamārthe, kulmāṣāṃścidāharetyavakutsite*) it is used in the sense of honour in the sentence "आचार्यश्चिद् (*ācāryaścid*)", in the sense of comparison in "dadhicit", and in the sense of reproach in "कुल्माषांश्चिद् (*kulmāṣāṃścid*)", and he explains the meaning. Here Sāyaṇa takes it in the sense of honour, and by describing the goddess Rodasī as venerable and imperishable, he describes the surpassing greatness of the Maruts through the greatness of that goddess.

##### Grammar

- विवक्कि—वच परिभाषणे धातुः । लट् उत्तमपुरुषैकवचने मिप् प्रत्ययः । बहुलं छंदसि इति श्लु विकरणम् । धातोः द्वित्वे आगते बहुलं छंदसि (पा. सू. ७-४-७८) इति अभ्यासस्य इत्वम् । छांदसत्वात् धातुचकारस्य कुत्वम् । तिजंतनिघातस्वरः भवति ।
- महिमा—मह शब्दात् अतिशयार्थे इवनिच् प्रत्ययः । चितः (पा. सू. ६-१-१६३) इति अंतोदात्तः भवति ।
- अस्ति—यद्योगत्वात् निघातस्वरः न भवति । तिप् पित्त्वात् धातुस्वरः अवतिष्ठते ।
- वृषमनाः—बहुव्रीहिसमासः । पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरः भवति ।
- अहंयुः—अहं इति मांतमव्ययम् । अहंकारार्थे प्रयुक्तम् । अहं शुभमोर्युस् (पा. सू. ५_२-४०) इति मत्त्वर्थे युस् प्रत्ययः । प्रत्ययस्वरेण अंतोदात्तः भवति ।
- स्थिरा—ष्ठा गतिनिवृत्तौ धातुः । अजिर शिशिर—(उ. सू. १-५३) इति किरच् प्रत्ययांततया निपातितम् । स्त्रीत्वे आदंतत्वात् टाप् । चित्त्वात् अंतोदात्तस्वरः भवति ।

### Printed p. 100
- जनीः—जन्यंते आसु इति जनयः, स्त्रियः । जनी प्रादुर्भावे धातुः । इन् सर्वधातुभ्यः इति इन्‌प्रत्ययः । इत्यादिर्नित्यं (पा. सू. ६-१-१६९) इति आद्युदात्तस्वरः भवति । द्वितीयाबहुवचनांतरूपम् ।
- वहते—वह प्रापणे धातुः । लट् प्रथमपुरुषैकवचनांतरूपम् । यद्योगत्वात् निघातस्वरः न भवति । तास्यनुदात्तेत्—(पा. सू. ६-१-१८६) इति सूत्रेण लसार्वधातुकस्य अनुदात्तत्वात् धातुस्वरः अवतिष्ठते ।
- सुभागाः—शोभनः भागः यासां ताः । नञ् सुभ्यां (पा. सू. ६-२-१७२) इति अंतोदात्तस्वरः भवति । ॥ ७ ॥

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> पांति॑ मि॒त्रावरु॑णावव॒द्याच्चय॑त ईमर्य॒मो अप्र॑शस्तान् ।  
> उ॒त च्य॑वंते॒ अच्यु॑ता ध्रु॒वाणि॑ वावृ॒ध ईं॑ मरुतो॒ दाति॑वारः ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पांति । मित्रावरुणौ । अवद्यात् । चयते । ईं । अर्यमो इति । अप्रशस्तान् ।  
> उत । च्यवंते । अच्युता । ध्रुवाणि । ववृधे । ईं । मरुतः । दातिवारः ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मरुतः युष्मत्संबंधिनो देवा मित्रावरुणावहोरात्र्यभिमानिदेवावर्यमो । अर्यमा उ इति निपातसमुदायात्मकमेकं पदं । उशब्दोऽपिशब्दार्थे । अर्यमापि । एते त्रयोऽपीममुं लोकं यज्ञं नावद्याद्गह्र्यादयथानुष्ठानात् । जगत्पक्षे व्यवहारप्रतिबंधरूपात् पांति । रक्षंति । तथैतेषामप्रशस्तान् क्षीणानामपि पदार्थांश्चयते । चातयंति । नाशयंति । उतापि चैतैः
> प्रेरितान्यच्युताच्युतान्यस्राविणि आत एवध्रुवाण्यविचलानि मेघस्थोदकानि च्यवंते ।
> च्यावयंति वैते । अहोरात्रे वै मित्रावरुणावहोरात्राभ्यां खलु वै पर्जन्यो
> वर्षतीति श्रुतेः । यद्वा । अच्युतानि ध्रुवाणि धनानि च्यवंते । कदेति उच्यते ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Maruts, the gods connected with you, Mitra and Varuṇa, the deities presiding over day and night, and Aryaman (अर्यमो (*aryamo*) is one word made of the particles अर्यमा उ (*aryamā u*); उ (*u*) has the sense of "also") — all three protect this world, the sacrifice, from what is blameworthy, that is, from improper performance; in the case of the world, from what obstructs worldly activity. Likewise they destroy (चयते (*cayate*) = चातयंति (*cātayaṃti*), नाशयंति (*nāśayaṃti*)) even the unpraiseworthy, wasted things of these. And also, moved by them, they cause the unfallen, that is, non-flowing and therefore firm, motionless waters in the cloud to fall; or they cause them to fall. Because of the śruti अहोरात्रे वै मित्रावरुणावहोरात्राभ्यां खलु वै पर्जन्यो वर्षतीति (*ahorātre vai mitrāvaruṇāvahorātrābhyāṃ khalu vai parjanyo varṣatīti*) (Mitra and Varuṇa are day and night; through day and night Parjanya rains). Or else: the unfallen, firm riches fall. When is this said?

### Printed p. 101

> हे मरुतः युष्मदीय ईमयं कालो दातिवारः प्रदेयजलः खंडितमेघो
> ना यदा ववृधे वर्धते । तदा मित्रादयो देवा युष्मदनुकूलव्यापारेण
> जगद्रक्षंतीत्यर्थः । यद्वा । दातिवारो दत्तवरणीयहविर्लक्षणधनो यजमानो
> हे मरुतः ईमेनान्युष्मान्ववृधे वर्धयति यदा तदेत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Maruts, when this time of yours, the water-giving one, with the cloud broken, increases — then Mitra and the other gods protect the world by activity favourable to you, is the meaning. Or else: when the sacrificer, दातिवारः (*dātivāraḥ*), who has given the desirable wealth in the form of oblation, increases you, O Maruts, then (the same follows), is the meaning.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (हे) मरुतः (*(he) marutaḥ*)—O Maruts
- *(yadā)*—when
- ईं (*īṃ*)—this time
- दातिवारः (*dātivāraḥ*)—as one possessing water to be given, or a broken cloud
- ववृधे (*vavṛdhe*)—grows; [or दातिवारः (*dātivāraḥ*)—the sacrificer endowed with wealth in the form of oblation, given (to you) and chosen (by you); ईं (*īṃ*)—you; ववृधे (*vavṛdhe*)—he increases]
- (तदा (*tadā*)—then)
- मित्रावरुणौ (*mitrāvaruṇau*)—Mitra and Varuṇa
- अर्यमो (*aryamo*)—Aryaman also
- ईं (*īṃ*)—this world or the sacrifice
- अवद्यात् (*avadyāt*)—from everything blameworthy
- पांति (*pāṃti*)—protect
- (तथा (*tathā*)—likewise)
- अप्रशस्तान् (*apraśastān*)—all unworthy things
- चयते (*cayate*)—they destroy
- उत (*uta*)—and
- अच्युता (*acyutā*)—not raining (and therefore)
- ध्रुवाणि (*dhruvāṇi*)—motionless waters in the clouds
- च्यवंते (*cyavaṃte*)—they shed; [or अच्युता (*acyutā*)—without fall (and therefore); ध्रुवाणि (*dhruvāṇi*)—firm riches; च्यवंते (*cyavaṃte*)—they rain down]

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Maruts, when the sacrificer, offering oblations and so on, increases you, your Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryaman protect our sacrifice from all faults, destroy everything unworthy, and bestow wealth and the like that is without fall and firm (in this world, destroying all wasted things, they shed the waters in the non-raining clouds and show favour).

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Mitra, and Varuna, and Aryaman, defend (this) sacrifice from (all that is) reprehensible, and destroy (all that are) unworthy. They cause the yet unfallen and showering (rains) to descend, when, Maruts, the water-yielding (season) gives increase to this (world).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

मित्रावरुण् आर्यनो ईं अवद्यात् पांति—अहोरात्र्यभिमानिदेवौ आर्यमापि एते त्रयः अमुं लोकं यज्ञं वा आयथानुष्ठानात् जगत्पक्षे (*mitrāvaruṇ āryano īṃ avadyāt pāṃti—ahorātryabhimānidevau āryamāpi ete trayaḥ amuṃ lokaṃ yajñaṃ vā āyathānuṣṭhānāt jagatpakṣe*)

### Printed p. 102
व्यवहारप्रतिबंधरूपात् रक्षंति (*vyavahārapratibaṃdharūpāt rakṣaṃti*). Yāska says that Mitra and Varuṇa, both together, have a joint praise (सहैव संस्तवः (*sahaiva saṃstavaḥ*)), a praise in one sentence. Here Mitra and Varuṇa are described as deities connected with the Maruts. These two together with the god Aryaman, three deities, protect this world and the sacrifice. Sāyaṇa explains that they protect so that there is no shortcoming of duty in the sacrifice, no deviation in duty, and no obstruction whatever in the world's activity. And he says that these two are the deities presiding over day and night.

अप्रशस्ता चयते - क्षीणानपि पदार्थान् चातॆयंति नाशयंति (*apraśastā cayate - kṣīṇānapi padārthān cātèyaṃti nāśayaṃti*). Since Mitra and Varuṇa preside over day and night, they bring about change of form in each thing according to time, so that these things come to destruction; this is the meaning given. Thus the power of acting as creators and destroyers is set out for Mitra and Varuṇa, presiding over day and night.

By the sentence अच्युता ध्रुवाणि च्यवंते (*acyutā dhruvāṇi cyavaṃte*) it is said that they also bring about maintenance. अच्युतानि (*acyutāni*) is explained as अस्रावीणि (*asrāvīṇi*): having attained a dense state and being liquid in form; being condensed they are ध्रुवाणि (*dhruvāṇi*), motionless; the waters in the cloud, these cause च्यावयंति (*cyāvayaṃti*) to fall on the earth as rain: this is one kind of meaning. यद्वा अच्युतानि ध्रुवाणि धनानि च्यवंते (*yadvā acyutāni dhruvāṇi dhanāni cyavaṃte*): they bestow imperishable wealth (on the earth) and bring about the carrying on of worldly activity; thus creation, maintenance and dissolution belong to Mitra and Varuṇa, presiding over day and night: this is another meaning described. Mitra and Varuṇa exist in the form of day and night, and rain occurs in day and night; on this point the śruti sentence “अहोरात्रे वै मित्रावरुणावहोरात्राभ्यां खलु वै पर्जन्यो वर्षति (*ahorātre vai mitrāvaruṇāvahorātrābhyāṃ khalu vai parjanyo varṣati*)” is cited.

Addressing the Maruts, the time at which these Mitra and other deities perform the world's creative works is set out. The sentence ईं दातिवारः ववृधे (*īṃ dātivāraḥ vavṛdhe*) teaches the time of favour of Mitra and the others. For the particle ईं (*īṃ*) the meaning "this time" is explained here. For the word दातिवार (*dātivāra*): खंडितमेघः प्रदेयजलः (*khaṃḍitameghaḥ pradeyajalaḥ*), when the time in which the water takes on the manner of raining, with density in the clouds reduced, has increased; and दत्तवरणीयहविर्लक्षणधनो यजमानः (*dattavaraṇīyahavirlakṣaṇadhano yajamānaḥ*) (offering oblations to the gods), the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice with wealth in the form of oblation desired by the gods: in accordance with the time of his offering for the gods, Mitra and the other gods, being favourable to the works to be performed by the Maruts, accomplish and protect the activities of the world; so it is described.

### Printed p. 103

##### Grammar

- पांति — पा रक्षणे । धातुः । लट् प्रथमपुरुषबहुवचनांतरूपं । पादादौ वर्तमानत्वान्निघातस्वरो न भवति । तिप् पित्त्वाद्धातुस्वरः शिष्यते ।
- आवद्यात् — नद व्यक्तायां वाचि । नञ् उपपदे अवद्यपण्यवर्या—(पा. सू. ३-१-१०१) इति यदंतत्वेन निपातितं । पंचम्येकवचनांतरूपं ।
- चयते — चिञ् चयने । धातुश्चुरादिः । धातो इत्वसामर्थ्याण्णिच् विकल्पेन भवति । व्यत्ययेन झ प्रत्ययस्य अतादेशः । शप् निमित्तत्वेन धातोरिको गुणोऽयादेशः । पादादौ वर्तमानत्वान्निघातस्वरो न भवति । धातुस्वरः शिष्यते ।
- अप्रशस्तान् — शंसु स्तुतौ । धातुः । कर्मणि निष्ठा प्रत्ययः । नञ् तत्पुरुषसमासः । तत्पुरुषे तु—(पा. सू. ६-२-२) इत्यव्ययपूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरो भवति ।
- च्यवंते — चुज् गतौ धातुः । लट् प्रथमपुरुषैकवचनांतरूपं । तिजंतनिघातस्वरो भवति ।
- अच्युता — न च्युतानि अच्युतानि । नञ् तत्पुरुषसमासः । पूर्ववन्नञ् स्वरः शिष्यते । शेश्छंदसि बहुलं (पा. सू. ६-१-७०) इति नपुंसकरूपे शेर्लोपः ।
- ववृधे — वृधु वृद्धौ । धातुः । छंदसि लुज् लज्—(पा. सू. ३-४-६) इति वर्तमानार्थे लिट् । प्रथमपुरुषैकवचनांतरूपं । पादस्यादौ वर्तमानत्वान्निघातस्वरो न भवति । प्रत्ययस्वरेणांतोदात्तो भवति ।
- दातिवारः — बहुव्रीहौ बहुव्रीहौ प्रकृत्या—(पा सू. ६-२-१) इति पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरो भवति । ॥ ८ ॥

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> न॒ही नु वो॑ मरुतो॒ अंत्य॒स्मे आ॒रात्ता॑च्चि॒च्छव॑सो॒ अंत॑मा॒पुः ।  
> ते धृ॒ष्णुना॒ शव॑सा शूशु॒वांसोऽर्णो॒ न द्वेषो॑ दृढ॒ता ॑ 
> परि ष्ठुः ॥ ९ ॥

### Printed p. 104

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> नहि । नु । वः । मरुतः । अंति । अस्मे इति । आरात्तात् । चित् । शवसः । अंतं । आपुः ।  
> ते । धृष्णुना । शवसा । शुशुवांसः । अर्ण॥ । न । द्वेषः । धृषता । परि । स्थुः ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मरुतः वः शवसो युष्माकं संबंधिनो बलस्यांतमवसानमंत्यंतिक
> आरात्ताच्चिद्दूरादपि । यद्वा । चिदिति कुत्सायां । अत्यंतदूरात् । अस्मे अस्मासु मध्य
> एकेऽपि नु क्षिप्रं नह्यापुः । नैव खलु प्राप्नुवंति । संनिधावसंनिधौ च न प्राप्नुवंतीत्यर्थः ! अथ परोक्षे प्राप्तबलवत्वमाह ! ते खलु मरुतो धृष्णुना धर्षकेण
> शवसा बलेन शूशुवांसो वर्धमानाः आत एव धृषता पराभिभावकेन सामर्थ्येन
> द्विषो द्वेषून्परि ष्टुः । परिभवंति । वशीकुर्वंति । तत्र दृष्टांतः । अर्णो
> नोदकमिव । यथोदकं स्वविरोधि रजआदिकमभिभवति तद्वत् । यस्मादेवं तस्माच्छवसो०तं नापुः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Maruts, no one among us has reached the limit (अंतं (*aṃtaṃ*)) of your strength (शवसः (*śavasaḥ*)), whether near (अंति (*aṃti*)) or far (आरात्तात् चित् (*ārāttāt cit*))." Alternatively, चित् (*cit*) expresses contempt, meaning "from exceedingly far". Not even one among us quickly attains it; they do not reach it whether in nearness or in non-nearness. Then Sāyaṇa states their strength, which is not directly perceived: those Maruts, growing by their daring (धृष्णुना (*dhṛṣṇunā*)) strength, and therefore by an overpowering ability, overcome (परि ष्टुः (*pari ṣṭuḥ*)), that is, subdue, their enemies. The example: like water (अर्णः न (*arṇaḥ na*)), which overcomes its opponents such as dust. Because this is so, the limit of their strength is not reached.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ते (*te*)—those Marut deities
- धृष्णुना (*dhṛṣṇunā*)—with daring
- शवसा (*śavasā*)—with their own strength
- शूशुवांसः (*śūśuvāṃsaḥ*)—growing
- दृषता (*dṛṣatā*)—(therefore) with overpowering ability
- द्वेषः (*dveṣaḥ*)—haters
- अर्णः न (*arṇaḥ na*)—as the water of the ocean covers dust and the like, so
- परि ष्टुः (*pari ṣṭuḥ*)—they defeat (bring under control); therefore
- (हे) मरुतः (*(he) marutaḥ*)—O Maruts
- वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- शवसः (*śavasaḥ*)—of strength
- अंतं (*aṃtaṃ*)—the end
- अंति (*aṃti*)—nearby
- आरात्तात् चित् (*ārāttāt cit*)—(or) from very far
- अस्मे (*asme*)—(among) us (no one)
- नु (*nu*)—quickly
- नहि आपुः (*nahi āpuḥ*)—attains not at all

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Maruts, growing by their natural strength, bring all haters under their control by their enemy-overpowering ability, as ocean water subdues dust and the like. Therefore no one can know the limit of their strength, whether from near or from far.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
None of us, Maruts, have attained, either nearly or remotely the limit of your strength: those (Maruts), increasing in energy and vigour, overwhelm their enemies like an ocean.

### Printed p. 105

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

शवसः अंतं अस्मे नु नहि आपुः (*śavasaḥ aṃtaṃ asme nu nahi āpuḥ*)—not even one among us quickly reaches the end of your strength; they reach it neither in nearness nor in non-nearness. The word शवस् (*śavas*) is listed among the names of water (Ni. 2-24) and read among the names of strength (Ni. 3-9). Since none of us seers, the beholders of the mantra, is able to know by knowledge the limit of the Maruts' strength, either in your presence or at a distance, their greatness is praised as being of immeasurable strength.

The author of the Nirukta explains that the word अस्मे (*asme*), while remaining of one form in all seven cases, conveys the sense of the respective case, and gives examples. In अस्मै ते बंधुः (*asmai te baṃdhuḥ*), अस्मे (*asme*) is given the nominative sense वयं (*vayaṃ*), "we". In अस्मे यातं नासत्या सजोषाः (*asme yātaṃ nāsatyā sajoṣāḥ*) the accusative sense अर्मान् (*armān*) is given, "us". In अस्मे समानेभिर्वृषभ पौंस्येभिः (*asme samānebhirvṛṣabha pauṃsyebhiḥ*) it is used in the instrumental sense अस्माभिः (*asmābhiḥ*), "by us". In अस्मे प्रयंधि मघवन्नृजीषिन् (*asme prayaṃdhi maghavannṛjīṣin*) the dative sense अस्मभ्यं (*asmabhyaṃ*), "for us", is described. In अस्मे आरात् चित् द्वेषः सनुतर्युयोतु (*asme ārāt cit dveṣaḥ sanutaryuyotu*) the ablative sense अस्मत् (*asmat*), "from us", is explained. In ऊर्व इव पप्रथे कामो अस्मे (*ūrva iva paprathe kāmo asme*) the genitive use अस्माकं (*asmākaṃ*), "our", is shown. In अस्मे धत्तवसवो वसूनि (*asme dhattavasavo vasūni*) the locative sense अस्मासु (*asmāsu*), "in our regard", is established here. (Ni. 6-7)

आराच्चित् (*ārāccit*)—दूरादपि । यद्वा । चिदिति कुत्सायां अत्यंतदूरात् (*dūrādapi | yadvā | ciditi kutsāyāṃ atyaṃtadūrāt*). The Nirukta author explains that the particle चित् (*cit*) is used in many senses: चिदित्येषोऽनेककर्मा (*cidityeṣo 'nekakarmā*). Here the word चित् (*cit*) is given the sense of अपि (*api*), that is, of combination, and also the sense of reproach; the meaning "whether far or very far" is explained.

शवसा शुशुवांसः (*śavasā śuśuvāṃsaḥ*)—the Maruts, growing by an ability that puts others down, keep their enemies द्वेषः परि ष्टुः (*dveṣaḥ pari ṣṭuḥ*) under their control by their own power. For this the simile अर्णः न (*arṇaḥ na*) is given: यथा उदकं रज आदिकं अभिभवति तद्वत् (*yathā udakaṃ raja ādikaṃ abhibhavati tadvat*). As water by its own ability absorbs into itself its enemy, dust, so they too keep enemies under their control. By this the sense is described that they are of immeasurable strength.

### Printed p. 106

##### Grammar

- नहि — निपातस्य च (पा. सू. ६-३-१३६) इति संहितायां दीर्घो भवति ।
- अंति — अंतिकशब्दः । कादिलोपो बहुलमिति वक्तव्यं इति वचनात् तस्य ककारस्य लोपो भवति ।
- अस्मे — सप्तमीबहुवचने परे सुपां सुलुक्—(पा. सू. ७-१-३९) इति तस्य शे आदेशः ।
- आरात्तात् — आरादिति दूरसमीपार्थवाचकमव्ययं । तस्मात् स्वार्थे तातिल् प्रत्ययः । लित्त्वात् तस्य पूर्वस्योदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- आपुः — आप्लृ व्याप्तौ । धातुः । छांदसत्वेन वर्तमानार्थे लिट् । प्रथमपुरुषबहुवचनांतरूपं । नहि इत्यस्य संबंधत्वान्निघातस्वरो न भवति । प्रत्ययस्वरेणांतोदात्तो भवति ।
- धृष्णुना — इधृषा प्रागल्भ्ये । धातुः । त्रसिगृधिधृषि क्षिपेः क्नुः (पा. सू. ३-२-१४०) इति क्नु प्रत्ययः । कित्त्वात् लघूपधगुणो न भवति । तृतीयैकवचनांतरूपं ।
- शूशुवांसः — टु ओश्वि गतिवृद्ध्योः धातुः । लिटः क्वसुरादेशः । विभाषा श्वेः (पा. सू. ६-१ ३०) इति धातोः संप्रसारणं पूर्वरूपं । वस्वेकाजाद्भसां (पा. सू. ७-२-६७) इति नियमात् प्रत्ययस्येडागमो न भवति । प्रत्ययनिमित्तत्वेन धातोर्द्वित्वे कृते तुजादीनां दीर्घो- (पा. सू. ६-१-७) इत्यभ्यासस्य दीर्घो भवति । प्रत्ययस्वरो भवति ।
- द्वेषः — द्विष अप्रीतौ धातुः । अन्येभ्योऽपि दृश्यंते इति विच् । पुगंत लघूपधस्य च इति लघूपधगुणः । प्रत्ययस्य सर्वलोपो भवति । द्वितीयाबहुवचनांतरूपं ।
- स्थुः — ष्ठा गतिनिवृत्तौ । धातुः । छंदसि लुज् लज् लिटः इति लुज् । प्रथमपुरुषबहुवचने झि प्रत्ययः । ज्लिगॆ सिचादेशः । आतः (पा. सू. ३-४-११०) इति झेर्जुसादेशः । गातिस्थाघु—(पा सू. २-४-७७) इति सिचो लुक् । उस्यपदांतात् इति पररूपं । बहुलं छंदस्यमाज्‌योगेऽपि इत्यडागमो न भवति । निघातस्वरो भवति । उपसर्गात् सुनो—(पा. सू. ८-२-६५) इति षत्वं भवति । ॥ ९ ॥**NOTE**

### Printed p. 107
#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> व॒यम॒द्येंद्र॑स्य॒ प्रेष्ठा॑ व॒यं श्वो वो॑चेमहि सम॒र्ये ।  
> व॒यं पु॒रा महि॑ च नो॒ अनु॒ द्यून्तन्न॑ ऋभु॒क्षा न॒रामनु॑  
> ष्यात् ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वयं । अद्य । इंद्रस्य । प्रेष्ठाः । वयं । श्वः । वोचेमहि । समर्ये ।  
> वयं । पुरा । महि । च । नः । अनु । द्यून् । तत् । नः । ऋभुक्षाः । नरां ।  
> अनु । स्यात् ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वयमद्येत्येतां दशमीमस्तमित आदित्ये जपेत् । तथा च सूत्रं ।
> वयमद्येंद्रस्य प्रेष्ठा इत्यस्तं यात्यादित्ये । आ. गृ. २-६-१४ । इति ॥
> अव्यास्मिन्निने वयं यष्टार इंद्रस्य देवस्य प्रेष्ठाः प्रियतमा भवेव ।
> यद्वा। प्रियतमा वयमिंद्रस्य महत्त्वं वोचेमहि। स्तोतुं समर्था भूयास्म।
> किंच वयं श्वः परस्मिन्नपि दिने समर्यॆ । सह मर्या मर्त्या ऋत्विजो यस्मिन् स
> समर्यो यज्ञः । तस्मिन् । संग्रामे वा । इंद्रस्य महत्त्वं वोचेमहि । किंच
> वयं पुरा पूर्वस्मिन्नतीतेऽपि काले महि महद्बलं नोऽस्मदर्थं वोचेमहि ।
> तथा द्यून्दिवसाननु । प्रतिदिवसमित्यर्थः । अतीत आगामिनि च दिवसे सर्वदा
> वोचेमहि । तत्तस्मादृभक्षाः । महन्नामैतत् ऋभुक्षा उक्षा इति तन्नामसु
